# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCIES MANDATES (COMPLETE)

## 844. STATE HOUSE HEADQUARTERS

**MDA Code:** 111001001

State House Headquarters is presidential residence and office complex in Abuja. Operates as seat of Nigerian presidency. Houses President's office and residence. Accommodates Vice President's office. Coordinates all State House operations and activities. Manages presidential protocols and ceremonies.

**Core Mandate:** To serve as presidential residence and office, coordinate State House operations, manage presidential protocols and support executive functions.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Serve as presidential residence and office complex
(b) House President's official office
(c) House President's official residence
(d) Accommodate Vice President's office
(e) Coordinate all State House operations
(f) Manage presidential protocols
(g) Manage State House ceremonies
(h) Provide presidential security coordination
(i) Manage State House administration
(j) Coordinate official state functions
(k) Host state banquets and receptions
(l) Accommodate visiting heads of state
(m) Manage State House staff and personnel
(n) Coordinate presidential engagements
(o) Manage State House facilities and infrastructure
(p) Provide presidential support services
(q) Coordinate State House communications
(r) Manage presidential protocol requirements
(s) Organize national ceremonies and events
(t) Advance executive operations through presidential residence and office complex coordination

## 783. OFFICE OF THE CHIEF OF STAFF TO THE PRESIDENT

**MDA Code:** 111001004

Office of the Chief of Staff to the President is top presidential coordination office managing presidency operations. Located in State House, Abuja, operating directly under Office of President. Established to coordinate presidency activities, manage presidential schedule and ensure effective executive operations. Coordinates activities of federal ministries, departments and agencies. Manages President's schedule, appointments and engagements. Coordinates policy implementation across government. Serves as liaison between President and federal agencies. Manages presidential communications and correspondence. Coordinates inter-ministerial meetings and committees. Ensures presidential directives are implemented. Manages access to President and gatekeeping functions. Coordinates presidential travels and state visits. Oversees State House administration and operations. Advises President on administrative and coordination matters. Collaborates with ministers and permanent secretaries. One of most powerful political positions - controls access to President. Faces challenges from bureaucratic inertia, coordination difficulties and balancing political/administrative roles. Critical for presidency effectiveness, policy coordination and ensuring presidential directives implemented across government.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate presidency operations, manage presidential schedule, ensure policy implementation, coordinate federal agencies and support effective executive leadership.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate activities of federal ministries, departments and agencies
(b) Manage President's daily schedule and appointments
(c) Coordinate policy implementation across government
(d) Serve as liaison between President and federal agencies
(e) Manage presidential communications and official correspondence
(f) Coordinate inter-ministerial meetings and cabinet committees
(g) Ensure presidential directives and decisions are implemented
(h) Manage access to President and gatekeeping responsibilities
(i) Coordinate presidential travels, state visits and international engagements
(j) Oversee State House administration and operations
(k) Advise President on administrative and coordination matters
(l) Collaborate with ministers on policy execution
(m) Coordinate with permanent secretaries on implementation
(n) Manage relationships between presidency and legislature
(o) Control one of most powerful political positions in government
(p) Address bureaucratic inertia delaying implementation
(q) Navigate coordination difficulties across agencies
(r) Balance political considerations with administrative efficiency
(s) Ensure coherent whole-of-government approach
(t) Advance presidential effectiveness and policy coordination ensuring directives implemented managing powerful gatekeeping role

## 784. OFFICE OF THE CHIEF SECURITY OFFICER TO THE PRESIDENT (CSO)

**MDA Code:** 111001005

Office of the Chief Security Officer to the President provides security for President and State House. Operating directly under Office of President. Established to ensure President's personal security, protect First Family and coordinate presidential security arrangements. Ensures President's personal security and protection. Protects First Family and presidential household. Coordinates presidential security during travels and engagements. Manages State House security and access control. Coordinates with DSS, police and military on presidential security. Ensures secure presidential movements and motorcades. Protects President during public appearances and events. Coordinates security for state functions and ceremonies. Manages presidential security intelligence and threat assessments. Operates presidential security detail and bodyguards. Ensures secure communications for President. Coordinates international security during presidential foreign trips. Collaborates with host countries' security during visits. Addresses challenges from evolving security threats, coordination across security agencies and balancing security with public access. Presidential security paramount given Nigeria's complex security environment. Critical for presidential safety, protecting democratic leadership and ensuring secure executive operations.

**Core Mandate:** To ensure President's personal security, protect First Family, coordinate presidential security arrangements and maintain State House security.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Ensure President's personal security and physical protection
(b) Protect First Family and presidential household members
(c) Coordinate presidential security during travels and public engagements
(d) Manage State House security and perimeter protection
(e) Control access to presidential areas and secure zones
(f) Coordinate with DSS on presidential security intelligence
(g) Collaborate with police on presidential security operations
(h) Work with military on presidential protection
(i) Ensure secure presidential movements and motorcades
(j) Protect President during public appearances and rallies
(k) Coordinate security for state functions and ceremonies
(l) Manage presidential security intelligence and threat assessments
(m) Operate presidential security detail and bodyguard teams
(n) Ensure secure communications and information security
(o) Coordinate international security during foreign presidential trips
(p) Collaborate with host countries' security services during visits
(q) Address evolving security threats to presidency
(r) Coordinate across multiple security agencies
(s) Balance tight security with democratic public access
(t) Advance presidential safety and security protecting democratic leadership in complex security environment

## 768. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR POLICY AND STRATEGIC STUDIES (NIPSS), KURU

**MDA Code:** 111006001

National Institute for Policy and Strategic Studies is premier federal think tank for strategic leadership and policy development. Located in Kuru, Plateau State, operating under Office of President. Established to train strategic leaders, conduct policy research and support national development planning. Conducts Senior Executive Course (SEC) for top government officials. Trains permanent secretaries, directors and senior civil servants. Provides strategic leadership and policy formulation training. Conducts research on national development issues. Studies security, economy, politics and social development. Develops national policies and strategic frameworks. Advises government on policy and strategy. Organizes policy dialogues and conferences. Publishes policy research and strategic papers. Trains military officers, judges and private sector executives. Promotes whole-of-government approach to development. Collaborates with international policy institutes. SEC graduates ("Kuru Men/Women") form Nigeria's strategic leadership network. Critical for strategic thinking, leadership development and policy formulation supporting national development despite research-policy implementation gaps.

**Core Mandate:** To train strategic leaders, conduct policy research, develop national strategies, advise government and build policy formulation capacity for national development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct Senior Executive Course (SEC) for strategic leadership
(b) Train permanent secretaries and top civil servants
(c) Provide strategic leadership and policy formulation training
(d) Train directors and senior government officials
(e) Conduct policy research on national development issues
(f) Study security, defense and strategic challenges
(g) Research economic development and growth strategies
(h) Analyze political development and governance
(i) Study social development and human capital issues
(j) Develop national policies and strategic frameworks
(k) Advise Federal Government on policy and strategy
(l) Organize policy dialogues, conferences and seminars
(m) Publish policy research papers and strategic studies
(n) Train military officers on strategic studies
(o) Provide training for judges and judicial officers
(p) Train private sector executives on national development
(q) Promote whole-of-government approach to challenges
(r) Collaborate with Harvard, Oxford and international policy institutes
(s) Build strategic leadership network ("Kuru Men/Women")
(t) Advance strategic thinking and policy development training Nigeria's top leadership supporting national development planning

## 748. NIGERIAN FINANCIAL INTELLIGENCE UNIT (NFIU)

**MDA Code:** 111009002

Nigerian Financial Intelligence Unit is autonomous federal agency combating money laundering, terrorism financing and financial crimes. Operating independently under NFIU Act. Established to analyze suspicious transactions, coordinate anti-money laundering efforts and support financial crimes investigations. Receives and analyzes suspicious transaction reports from financial institutions. Combats money laundering and illicit financial flows. Addresses terrorism financing and terrorist fund transfers. Analyzes currency transaction reports from banks. Collaborates with EFCC and ICPC on financial crimes. Shares financial intelligence with law enforcement. Coordinates with Egmont Group on international financial intelligence. Monitors politically exposed persons (PEPs) transactions. Addresses corruption proceeds and asset recovery. Implements international anti-money laundering standards. Autonomous from Central Bank (formerly under CBN). Addresses challenges from sophisticated money laundering, weak enforcement, limited asset recovery and banks' compliance gaps. Nigeria loses billions to illicit financial flows annually. Critical for anti-money laundering, terrorism financing prevention and financial crimes combat despite enforcement challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To combat money laundering and terrorism financing, analyze suspicious transactions, coordinate financial intelligence and support financial crimes investigations.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Combat money laundering and illicit financial flows
(b) Address terrorism financing and terrorist fund transfers
(c) Receive suspicious transaction reports from banks and financial institutions
(d) Analyze currency transaction reports exceeding thresholds
(e) Provide financial intelligence to law enforcement agencies
(f) Collaborate with EFCC on money laundering investigations
(g) Coordinate with ICPC on corruption-related financial crimes
(h) Share intelligence with DSS on terrorism financing
(i) Monitor politically exposed persons (PEPs) transactions
(j) Address corruption proceeds and stolen assets
(k) Support asset recovery and confiscation
(l) Implement Financial Action Task Force (FATF) recommendations
(m) Coordinate with Egmont Group on international financial intelligence
(n) Operate autonomously independent from Central Bank
(o) Analyze complex money laundering schemes
(p) Address trade-based money laundering
(q) Monitor cross-border financial transactions
(r) Ensure banks' anti-money laundering compliance
(s) Address Nigeria's illicit financial flows (billions lost annually)
(t) Advance anti-money laundering and terrorism financing prevention through financial intelligence despite sophisticated laundering and enforcement challenges

---

## 713. NIGERIA EXTRACTIVE INDUSTRIES TRANSPARENCY INITIATIVE (NEITI)

**MDA Code:** 111011001

Nigeria Extractive Industries Transparency Initiative is independent federal agency promoting transparency and accountability in oil, gas and mining sectors. Operating under Presidency. Established 2007 as Nigeria's implementing body for global Extractive Industries Transparency Initiative (EITI). Conducts audits of oil, gas and mining revenues. Promotes transparency in extractive industries payments. Publishes reconciliation reports on government oil revenues. Monitors oil companies' payments to government. Tracks mining sector revenues and payments. Addresses revenue leakages and underpayments. Promotes accountability in petroleum sector management. Publishes beneficial ownership information on oil companies. Conducts policy advocacy on extractive industries governance. Engages civil society and media on transparency. Collaborates with NNPC, FIRS and revenue agencies. Nigeria pioneer EITI implementer globally. NEITI reports exposed billions in revenue losses, unremitted funds. Faces challenges from limited enforcement powers (can only recommend), political interference and industry resistance. NEITI exposes but cannot compel action. Critical for transparency, accountability and preventing resource curse through extractive industries oversight despite limited enforcement authority.

**Core Mandate:** To promote transparency in extractive industries, audit oil and mining revenues, expose revenue leakages, publish reconciliation reports and advance accountability in petroleum and mining sectors.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Promote transparency and accountability in extractive industries
(b) Conduct audits of oil, gas and mining sector revenues
(c) Publish reconciliation reports on government oil revenues
(d) Monitor oil companies' payments to government
(e) Track mining sector revenues and royalty payments
(f) Address revenue leakages and underpayments
(g) Expose unremitted oil revenues to government
(h) Publish beneficial ownership information on oil companies
(i) Promote transparency in petroleum sector management
(j) Conduct policy advocacy on extractive industries governance
(k) Engage civil society on revenue transparency
(l) Collaborate with media on investigative reporting
(m) Coordinate with NNPC on oil revenue reconciliation
(n) Work with FIRS on tax revenues from extractive sectors
(o) Implement global EITI standards in Nigeria
(p) Publish annual NEITI reports exposing revenue issues
(q) Address billions in exposed revenue losses
(r) Advocate for reforms in petroleum fiscal regime
(s) Promote Petroleum Industry Act transparency provisions
(t) Advance extractive industries accountability through audits and transparency despite limited enforcement powers exposing revenue leakages and promoting petroleum sector reforms

---

## 707. NIGERIA ATOMIC ENERGY COMMISSION (NAEC) AND CENTRES

**MDA Code:** 111048001

Nigeria Atomic Energy Commission is federal agency developing peaceful nuclear energy applications and radiation safety. Operating under Federal Ministry of Science and Technology. Established to develop nuclear science, radiation technology and prepare for nuclear power. Conducts nuclear science research and development. Promotes peaceful uses of nuclear energy. Develops radiation technology applications for medicine, agriculture and industry. Operates research reactors (miniature neutron source reactor). Provides radiation safety and nuclear security services. Regulates radioactive materials and radiation sources. Trains nuclear scientists and radiation protection officers. Develops nuclear medicine and radiotherapy applications. Promotes nuclear agriculture using irradiation and isotopes. Researches nuclear power technology. Prepares Nigeria for potential nuclear power plants. Collaborates with IAEA on nuclear technology. Operates Centre for Energy Research and Training (CERT) Zaria. Addresses challenges from public nuclear fears, limited funding, nuclear technology restrictions and skills gaps. Nigeria exploring nuclear power for electricity generation. Critical for nuclear science development, radiation applications and building nuclear technology capacity for potential nuclear energy program.

**Core Mandate:** To develop peaceful nuclear energy applications, promote radiation technology, ensure nuclear safety, conduct nuclear research and prepare Nigeria for nuclear power.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Develop peaceful uses of nuclear energy and atomic energy
(b) Conduct nuclear science research and development
(c) Promote radiation technology applications in medicine, agriculture and industry
(d) Operate research reactors and nuclear facilities
(e) Provide radiation safety and protection services
(f) Regulate radioactive materials and radiation sources
(g) Ensure nuclear security and safeguards
(h) Train nuclear scientists and radiation protection officers
(i) Develop nuclear medicine and radiotherapy applications
(j) Promote nuclear agriculture using irradiation
(k) Use isotopes and radiation for pest control
(l) Research nuclear power technology
(m) Prepare Nigeria for potential nuclear power plants
(n) Collaborate with IAEA on nuclear technology transfer
(o) Operate Centre for Energy Research and Training (CERT) Zaria
(p) Monitor radiation exposure and environmental radioactivity
(q) Develop nuclear analytical techniques
(r) Address public concerns about nuclear safety
(s) Build nuclear technology capacity and skills
(t) Advance nuclear science and radiation technology developing peaceful applications preparing for nuclear power addressing energy crisis despite public fears and technology restrictions

## 588. NATIONAL AGRICULTURAL LAND DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY (NALDA)

**MDA Code:** 111056001

National Agricultural Land Development Authority is federal agency developing agricultural lands, irrigation and farm infrastructure. Operating under Office of President (previously Federal Ministry of Agriculture). Re-established/revitalized in 2020 to promote large-scale farming and address food security. Develops and reclaims agricultural lands for cultivation. Establishes integrated farms demonstrating modern agriculture. Develops irrigation infrastructure for all-season farming. Promotes mechanized agriculture and large-scale farming. Establishes Young Farmers Scheme training youth in agriculture. Provides agricultural inputs and extension services. Develops commodity value chains including rice, cassava, aquaculture and livestock. Establishes processing facilities for agricultural products. Coordinates with CBN Anchor Borrowers Program. Addresses food security and agricultural unemployment. Collaborates with state governments establishing farm estates. Faces challenges from land acquisition, funding and sustainability of established farms. Represents government direct intervention in agricultural production beyond traditional extension approach.

**Core Mandate:** To develop agricultural lands, establish integrated farms, promote mechanized agriculture, train young farmers and directly boost food production through government-led farming initiatives.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Develop and reclaim agricultural lands for cultivation
(b) Establish integrated farms across states demonstrating modern agriculture
(c) Develop irrigation infrastructure enabling dry season farming
(d) Promote mechanized agriculture using tractors and modern equipment
(e) Establish large-scale commodity farms for rice, maize and other crops
(f) Operate Young Farmers Scheme training youth in agriculture
(g) Provide agricultural inputs including seeds, fertilizers and chemicals
(h) Offer agricultural extension services and technical support
(i) Develop rice value chain including production, processing and marketing
(j) Promote cassava production and processing for industrial use
(k) Establish aquaculture farms for fish production
(l) Develop livestock farms for poultry, cattle and small ruminants
(m) Establish processing facilities for agricultural products
(n) Create farm estates and agricultural settlements
(o) Coordinate with CBN Anchor Borrowers Program on input supply
(p) Address youth unemployment through agricultural training and employment
(q) Promote food security through increased domestic production
(r) Collaborate with state governments on land allocation
(s) Establish demonstration farms showcasing best practices
(t) Advance food production and agricultural transformation through direct government farming initiatives, mechanization and youth engagement reducing food imports

## 583. NATIONAL AGENCY FOR SCIENCE AND ENGINEERING INFRASTRUCTURE (NASENI)

**MDA Code:** 111057001

National Agency for Science and Engineering Infrastructure (NASENI) is federal agency developing science, engineering and technology infrastructure for Nigeria's industrialization. Headquartered in Abuja with institutes and centers nationwide, operating under Office of President. Established by NASENI Act to build local capacity in engineering, manufacturing and technology. Operates multiple specialized institutes including Machine Tools and Equipment Center, Advanced Engineering Technology Center, Electronics Development Institute, Energy and Power Development Institute, Solar Energy Research Center and others. Develops indigenous capacity in machine tools manufacturing, electronics, renewable energy, biotechnology and engineering. Promotes local content and Made-in-Nigeria products. Conducts applied research translating to commercial products. Develops prototypes and pilot production systems. Provides engineering solutions for Nigerian industries. Trains engineers and technicians in advanced technologies. Collaborates with industries on technology transfer. Addresses challenges from inadequate funding, limited commercialization and foreign technology dependence. Critical for industrial development, technological self-reliance and engineering capacity building.

**Core Mandate:** To develop science and engineering infrastructure, build local technological capacity, promote indigenous manufacturing and advance Nigeria's industrialization and technological self-reliance.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Develop science and engineering infrastructure for Nigerian industrialization
(b) Operate Machine Tools and Equipment Development Center
(c) Run Advanced Engineering Technology Center for industrial engineering
(d) Manage Electronics Development Institute for local electronics manufacturing
(e) Operate Energy and Power Development Institute
(f) Run Solar Energy Research Center for renewable energy
(g) Manage Biotechnology Development Center
(h) Develop indigenous machine tools manufacturing capacity
(i) Promote local electronics and electrical equipment production
(j) Advance renewable energy technologies and solar power
(k) Conduct applied research translating to commercial products
(l) Develop prototypes and pilot production systems
(m) Promote Made-in-Nigeria products and local content
(n) Provide engineering solutions for Nigerian industries
(o) Train engineers and technicians in advanced technologies
(p) Transfer technology to local manufacturers
(q) Collaborate with universities on engineering research
(r) Support SMEs with technical assistance and equipment
(s) Develop appropriate technology for Nigerian context
(t) Advance industrial development, technological self-reliance and engineering capacity reducing foreign technology dependence and building Made-in-Nigeria manufacturing

## 614. NATIONAL COUNCIL ON CLIMATE CHANGE (NCCC)

**MDA Code:** 111058001

National Council on Climate Change is federal agency coordinating Nigeria's climate change response and climate policy. Operating under Office of President (previously under Federal Ministry of Environment). Established to coordinate climate change mitigation, adaptation and national climate action. Develops national climate change policy and strategies. Coordinates implementation of Nigeria's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) under Paris Agreement. Promotes climate change mitigation reducing greenhouse gas emissions. Develops climate adaptation programs protecting vulnerable communities. Coordinates access to climate finance from Green Climate Fund and international sources. Mainstreams climate change into sector policies and planning. Promotes renewable energy and clean energy transition. Addresses deforestation and promotes reforestation. Conducts climate vulnerability assessments. Promotes climate-smart agriculture. Coordinates climate research and data collection. Provides climate change awareness and education. Collaborates with UNFCCC and international climate agencies. Addresses challenges from limited climate finance, implementation gaps and balancing development with emissions reduction. Nigeria vulnerable to climate impacts including flooding, droughts, desertification and sea level rise affecting coastal cities.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate climate change response, implement NDCs, promote climate adaptation and mitigation, access climate finance and mainstream climate action across Nigeria's development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate Nigeria's climate change response and climate policy
(b) Develop national climate change policy and strategies
(c) Implement Nigeria's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs)
(d) Coordinate Paris Agreement implementation
(e) Promote greenhouse gas emissions reduction
(f) Develop climate change adaptation programs
(g) Protect vulnerable communities from climate impacts
(h) Access climate finance from Green Climate Fund
(i) Mobilize international climate finance for Nigeria
(j) Mainstream climate change into sector policies
(k) Promote renewable energy and clean energy transition
(l) Address deforestation and land degradation
(m) Promote reforestation and afforestation programs
(n) Conduct climate vulnerability and risk assessments
(o) Promote climate-smart agriculture
(p) Address coastal erosion and sea level rise threats
(q) Coordinate climate research and monitoring
(r) Provide climate change education and awareness
(s) Collaborate with UNFCCC and international climate agencies
(t) Advance climate resilience and low-carbon development protecting Nigeria from flooding, droughts and climate impacts while pursuing sustainable development

## 820. PRESIDENTIAL ECONOMIC ADVISORY COUNCIL

**MDA Code:** 111062001

Presidential Economic Advisory Council is expert panel of distinguished economists, finance experts, business leaders and development economists appointed by President. Typically comprises 8-12 members including former central bank governors. Provides independent economic advice and recommendations on fiscal and monetary policies. Guides Nigeria's economic development strategy and assesses economic performance.

(r) Recommend trade and export promotion policies
(s) Advise on financial sector stability

## 735. NIGERIAN ARMY

**MDA Code:** 116003001

Nigerian Army is land-based military service providing ground forces for national defense and internal security. Operating under Federal Ministry of Defence. Established to defend Nigeria's territorial integrity, conduct ground military operations and support internal security. Conducts counter-insurgency operations against Boko Haram in northeast. Combats banditry and kidnapping in northwest. Addresses IPOB agitations and security in southeast. Provides internal security support nationwide. Conducts peacekeeping missions under UN and AU (Mali, Liberia, Sudan, CAR). Operates across all states through divisions and brigades. Trains infantry, artillery, armor and special forces. Provides disaster relief and humanitarian assistance. Collaborates with Air Force and Navy on joint operations. Faces challenges from multiple simultaneous internal security operations, equipment shortages, funding constraints and soldiers' welfare issues. Army overstretched - fighting insurgency, banditry, kidnapping, herder conflicts simultaneously. Largest service branch with over 200,000 personnel. Critical for territorial defense, counter-insurgency and internal security despite operational overstretch.

**Core Mandate:** To defend Nigeria's territory, conduct ground military operations, lead counter-insurgency, provide internal security and deliver land-based military capabilities.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Defend Nigeria's territorial integrity and sovereignty
(b) Conduct counter-insurgency operations against Boko Haram in northeast
(c) Combat banditry and kidnapping in northwestern Nigeria
(d) Address IPOB separatist agitations in southeast
(e) Provide internal security support across Nigeria
(f) Conduct peacekeeping missions under UN mandates
(g) Participate in AU peace operations (Mali, CAR, Sudan)
(h) Operate divisions and brigades across all states
(i) Train infantry, artillery and armored units
(j) Develop special forces for counter-terrorism
(k) Provide disaster relief and humanitarian assistance
(l) Conduct flood response and emergency operations
(m) Collaborate with Air Force on joint air-ground operations
(n) Coordinate with Navy on coastal and riverine security
(o) Address multiple simultaneous security theaters
(p) Manage operational overstretch from internal security demands
(q) Address equipment shortages and aging platforms
(r) Ensure soldiers' welfare and motivation
(s) Train over 200,000 personnel
(t) Advance territorial defense and internal security through ground operations conducting counter-insurgency despite operational overstretch fighting multiple simultaneous threats

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 761-780

## 761. NIGERIAN NAVY (NN)

**MDA Code:** 116004001

Nigerian Navy is maritime military service protecting territorial waters, economic zones and providing naval defense. Operating under Federal Ministry of Defence. Established to defend Nigeria's maritime domain, protect offshore assets and secure sea lines of communication. Defends Nigerian territorial waters and Exclusive Economic Zone (EEZ). Protects offshore oil installations and platforms from attacks. Combats oil theft, illegal bunkering and pipeline vandalism at sea. Addresses piracy and maritime criminality in Gulf of Guinea. Provides maritime security and coastal patrol. Operates naval bases including Apapa, Calabar, Port Harcourt, Warri. Conducts anti-piracy operations in territorial waters. Collaborates with multinational maritime forces. Supports fisheries protection preventing illegal fishing. Conducts search and rescue operations at sea. Operates naval ships, patrol boats and aviation assets. Trains naval personnel and marine commandos. Faces challenges from aging fleet, limited modern platforms, oil theft costing billions and Gulf of Guinea piracy (once world's worst). Recent ship acquisitions improving capacity. Critical for maritime security, oil infrastructure protection and securing Nigeria's vast coastal waters despite fleet limitations.

**Core Mandate:** To defend maritime territory, protect offshore oil assets, combat piracy and oil theft, ensure maritime security and provide naval power for coastal defense.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Defend Nigerian territorial waters and maritime boundaries
(b) Protect Exclusive Economic Zone (EEZ) and continental shelf
(c) Guard offshore oil installations and platforms from attacks
(d) Combat oil theft, illegal bunkering and pipeline vandalism at sea
(e) Address piracy in Gulf of Guinea waters
(f) Conduct anti-piracy operations protecting shipping
(g) Provide maritime security and coastal patrol
(h) Operate naval bases (Apapa, Calabar, Port Harcourt, Warri)
(i) Collaborate with multinational maritime task forces
(j) Support fisheries protection preventing illegal fishing
(k) Conduct search and rescue operations at sea
(l) Operate naval ships, frigates and patrol boats
(m) Deploy Special Boat Service (SBS) commandos
(n) Provide riverine patrol in Niger Delta creeks
(o) Train naval personnel and officers
(p) Address aging naval fleet and equipment
(q) Combat oil theft costing Nigeria billions annually
(r) Address Gulf of Guinea piracy (formerly world's worst)
(s) Acquire modern naval platforms and vessels
(t) Advance maritime security protecting offshore oil infrastructure combating piracy and theft despite fleet limitations and Gulf of Guinea piracy challenges

## 633. NATIONAL HYDROGRAPHIC AGENCY

**MDA Code:** 116004002

National Hydrographic Agency is specialized federal agency conducting hydrographic surveys and maritime charting. Operating under Nigerian Navy and Federal Ministry of Defence. Established to map Nigeria's waters, conduct marine surveys and produce nautical charts. Conducts hydrographic surveys of Nigerian territorial waters, coastal areas and inland waterways. Produces nautical charts for safe navigation. Maps seabed topography and underwater features. Conducts bathymetric surveys measuring water depths. Supports maritime safety through accurate charts. Provides data for port development and dredging projects. Conducts surveys for offshore oil and gas operations. Supports maritime boundary delimitation. Collaborates with International Hydrographic Organization. Monitors coastal erosion and shoreline changes. Supports maritime domain awareness. Provides data for marine spatial planning. Addresses challenges from limited survey vessels, inadequate equipment and vast maritime area requiring mapping. Nigeria has extensive coastline (~850 km) and territorial waters requiring comprehensive surveys. Accurate charts critical for shipping safety, preventing groundings and maritime navigation. Supports growing maritime economy.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct hydrographic surveys, produce nautical charts, map Nigerian waters, support maritime safety and provide data for maritime development and navigation.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct hydrographic surveys of Nigerian territorial waters
(b) Survey coastal areas and shoreline mapping
(c) Conduct surveys of inland waterways and rivers
(d) Produce nautical charts for maritime navigation
(e) Map seabed topography and underwater features
(f) Conduct bathymetric surveys measuring water depths
(g) Update navigational charts ensuring accuracy
(h) Support maritime safety through chart production
(i) Provide data for port development projects
(j) Support dredging operations with survey data
(k) Conduct surveys for offshore oil and gas operations
(l) Support pipeline route surveys
(m) Assist maritime boundary delimitation
(n) Monitor coastal erosion and shoreline changes
(o) Support maritime domain awareness
(p) Provide data for marine spatial planning
(q) Collaborate with International Hydrographic Organization
(r) Train hydrographic surveyors
(s) Operate survey vessels and equipment
(t) Advance maritime safety and development through comprehensive hydrographic surveys producing accurate nautical charts supporting shipping, offshore oil operations and maritime navigation

## 733. NIGERIAN AIR FORCE (NAF)

**MDA Code:** 116005001

Nigerian Air Force is military service providing air power, aerial defense and aviation support. Operating under Federal Ministry of Defence. Established to defend Nigerian airspace, provide air power and support joint military operations. Defends Nigerian airspace from external threats. Conducts air strikes against Boko Haram and ISWAP insurgents. Provides close air support for ground troops. Operates fighter jets, attack helicopters and transport aircraft. Conducts intelligence, surveillance and reconnaissance (ISR) missions. Provides airlift for troops and supplies. Conducts search and rescue operations. Trains pilots, engineers and aviation personnel. Operates airbases nationwide including Kaduna, Kano, Maiduguri, Port Harcourt. Collaborates with Army and Navy on joint operations. Faces challenges from aging aircraft, limited modern platforms, maintenance difficulties and insurgency operations. Recent aircraft acquisitions (Super Tucano) enhanced counter-insurgency. Air Force operations critical in northeastern counter-insurgency. Critical for air defense, counter-insurgency operations and providing air power supporting Nigeria's military operations.

**Core Mandate:** To defend Nigerian airspace, provide air power, conduct aerial operations, support counter-insurgency and deliver aviation capabilities for national defense.

**Statutory Functions:**

(b) Conduct air strikes against Boko Haram and ISWAP insurgents
(c) Provide close air support for ground military operations
(d) Conduct bombing missions on terrorist camps and hideouts
(e) Operate fighter jets for air defense and combat
(f) Deploy attack helicopters for counter-insurgency operations
(g) Conduct intelligence, surveillance and reconnaissance (ISR) missions
(h) Provide airlift for troops, equipment and supplies
(i) Conduct search and rescue operations
(j) Train pilots, navigators and aircrew
(k) Train aviation engineers and technicians
(l) Operate airbases (Kaduna, Kano, Maiduguri, Port Harcourt, others)
(m) Maintain and service military aircraft and helicopters
(n) Collaborate with Army on joint ground-air operations
(o) Coordinate with Navy on maritime air patrols
(p) Acquire modern aircraft including Super Tucano platforms
(q) Address aging aircraft and maintenance challenges
(r) Conduct air evacuation for casualties
(s) Provide VIP transport for government officials
(t) Advance air power and national defense through aerial operations supporting counter-insurgency in northeast providing critical air strikes and ISR

## 742. NIGERIAN DEFENCE ACADEMY (NDA), KADUNA

**MDA Code:** 116006001

Nigerian Defence Academy is premier tri-service military university training commissioned officers for armed forces. Located in Kaduna, operating under Federal Ministry of Defence. Established 1964 as Nigeria's foremost military academy combining military and academic education. Trains cadets for Army, Navy and Air Force simultaneously (tri-service). Provides undergraduate degree programs alongside military training. Offers programs in engineering, sciences, social sciences and humanities. Produces commissioned officers as Second Lieutenants, Sub-Lieutenants and Flying Officers. Provides military science and leadership training. Conducts physical training and military drill. Teaches warfare tactics and military strategy. Combines academic rigor with military discipline. Operates as degree-awarding institution. Trains Nigeria's future military leaders and commanders. Collaborates with civilian universities on academics. Sandhurst-type academy model. Faces challenges from funding constraints, infrastructure needs and balancing academic vs. military focus. NDA graduates form officer corps backbone. Critical for military leadership development, professional officer training and producing educated military commanders.

**Core Mandate:** To train commissioned military officers, provide tri-service military education, combine academic degrees with military training and develop Nigeria's future military leadership.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Train commissioned officers for Nigerian Army, Navy and Air Force
(b) Provide tri-service military education simultaneously
(c) Offer undergraduate degree programs in engineering disciplines
(d) Provide sciences, social sciences and humanities education
(e) Produce commissioned officers as Second Lieutenants (Army)
(f) Graduate Sub-Lieutenants for Nigerian Navy
(g) Train Flying Officers for Nigerian Air Force
(h) Provide military science and leadership education
(i) Conduct physical training and military discipline
(j) Teach warfare tactics and military strategy
(k) Combine academic rigor with military standards
(l) Operate as degree-awarding military university
(m) Train future military leaders and senior commanders
(n) Provide character development and ethics training
(o) Collaborate with civilian universities on academic programs
(p) Maintain Sandhurst-type military academy standards
(q) Conduct joint military exercises and training
(r) Address funding constraints and infrastructure needs
(s) Balance academic excellence with military proficiency
(t) Advance military leadership development producing professional officers combining academic education with military training forming armed forces officer corps backbone

## 617. NATIONAL DEFENCE COLLEGE (NDC)

**MDA Code:** 116007001

National Defence College is premier strategic leadership institution providing senior military and civilian leadership training. Located in Abuja, operating under Ministry of Defence. Established to train senior military officers, senior civil servants and private sector leaders on national security, defense strategy and strategic leadership. Provides Senior Course for Colonels/Brigadiers, equivalent civil servants and private sector executives. Covers national security strategy, defense policy and strategic studies. Promotes civil-military understanding and cooperation. Conducts research on security challenges and strategic issues. Trains on crisis management and decision-making. Studies geopolitics and international relations. Promotes whole-of-government approach to security. Graduates include future generals, permanent secretaries and CEOs. Collaborates with international war colleges and defense institutions. Conducts security seminars and strategic dialogue. Addresses challenges from evolving security threats (insurgency, terrorism, cybersecurity). Critical for strategic leadership development, national security planning and preparing Nigeria's future leaders for complex security environment. Equivalent to war colleges in other countries.

**Core Mandate:** To provide strategic leadership training for senior military and civilian leaders, promote civil-military relations, conduct security research and develop national security expertise.

(b) Train senior civil servants on national security and defense
(c) Educate private sector executives on strategic security issues

(e) Teach national security strategy and policy formulation
(f) Cover defense planning and military strategy
(g) Promote civil-military understanding and cooperation
(h) Conduct research on security challenges and threats
(i) Study terrorism, insurgency and asymmetric warfare
(j) Teach crisis management and strategic decision-making
(k) Cover geopolitics, international relations and diplomacy
(l) Promote whole-of-government approach to security

(q) Collaborate with international war colleges
(r) Host international participants promoting defense diplomacy
(s) Develop future military and civilian security leaders
(t) Advance strategic leadership and national security expertise preparing generals, permanent secretaries and leaders for Nigeria's complex security environment

## 734. NIGERIAN ARMED FORCES RESETTLEMENT CENTRE, LAGOS

**MDA Code:** 116009001

Nigerian Armed Forces Resettlement Centre is federal institution providing skills training and transition support for retiring military personnel. Located in Lagos, operating under Federal Ministry of Defence. Established to prepare military personnel for civilian life after service. Provides vocational training for retiring soldiers, sailors and airmen. Offers skills acquisition in trades (welding, carpentry, plumbing, electricals). Provides entrepreneurship and business management training. Trains in agriculture and farming for post-military careers. Offers computer and ICT skills training. Provides guidance counseling and career planning. Supports military-to-civilian transition. Addresses unemployment among ex-servicemen. Provides starter packs and equipment for trainees. Promotes self-employment and business startups. Collaborates with industries on graduate placement. Addresses challenges from limited funding, inadequate facilities and ex-servicemen unemployment. Military retirees often struggle with civilian transition. Many veterans unemployed or underemployed. Critical for military welfare, successful transition and preventing disgruntled veterans from security risks through productive reintegration.

(f) Provide agriculture and farming training for post-military careers

(n) Provide catering and hospitality training
(o) Collaborate with industries on ex-servicemen employment
(p) Address limited funding and facility constraints
(q) Promote productive reintegration preventing veteran unemployment

(s) Prevent disgruntled veterans from security risks
(t) Advance military welfare ensuring successful civilian transition through skills training supporting self-employment preventing veteran unemployment and security risks

## 766. NIGERIANS IN DIASPORA COMMISSION (NIDCOM)

**MDA Code:** 119001002

Nigerians in Diaspora Commission is federal agency engaging Nigerian diaspora and harnessing diaspora contributions. Operating under Office of President. Established 2017 to coordinate diaspora engagement, promote diaspora investment and support Nigerians abroad. Engages Nigerian diaspora communities globally (estimated 15-17 million). Promotes diaspora investment in Nigeria. Facilitates diaspora remittances (over $20 billion annually). Supports diaspora business and entrepreneurship. Addresses welfare of Nigerians abroad. Coordinates diaspora return and resettlement programs. Promotes skills transfer from diaspora professionals. Organizes diaspora conferences and engagement. Addresses challenges facing Nigerians in diaspora. Collaborates with embassies on consular services. Promotes diaspora voting rights. Supports dual citizenship issues. Addresses challenges from limited diaspora policy implementation, bureaucratic barriers and weak diaspora-Nigeria linkages. Diaspora remittances exceed FDI but potential underutilized. Brain drain vs. brain gain debates. Critical for diaspora engagement, remittance facilitation and harnessing diaspora contributions despite limited policy implementation.

**Core Mandate:** To engage Nigerian diaspora, promote diaspora investment and remittances, support Nigerians abroad, facilitate skills transfer and harness diaspora contributions to development.

(b) Promote diaspora investment in Nigerian economy
(c) Facilitate diaspora remittances exceeding $20 billion annually
(d) Support diaspora business and entrepreneurship in Nigeria
(e) Address welfare and challenges of Nigerians abroad
(f) Coordinate diaspora return and resettlement programs
(g) Promote skills and knowledge transfer from diaspora professionals
(h) Organize diaspora investment conferences and summits

(j) Address discrimination and challenges facing diaspora

(r) Strengthen diaspora-Nigeria economic linkages
(s) Address brain drain while promoting brain gain
(t) Advance diaspora engagement harnessing $20B+ annual remittances and diaspora expertise despite policy implementation gaps

## 753. NIGERIAN INSTITUTE OF INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS (NIIA), LAGOS

**MDA Code:** 119008001

Nigerian Institute of International Affairs is premier foreign policy think tank conducting international relations research. Located in Lagos, operating under Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Established to research international relations, analyze foreign policy and provide diplomatic expertise. Conducts research on Nigerian foreign policy. Studies international relations and global affairs. Researches regional integration and African affairs. Analyzes diplomatic relations and international cooperation. Studies global trade and economic diplomacy. Researches peacekeeping and conflict resolution. Provides foreign policy analysis and recommendations. Trains diplomats and foreign service officers. Organizes international conferences and dialogues. Publishes research on international affairs. Advises government on foreign policy issues. Collaborates with international think tanks. ## 795. PERMANENT MISSION TO ECOWAS, ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 119009122

Permanent Mission to ECOWAS is Nigerian diplomatic representation to Economic Community of West African States. Located in Abuja (ECOWAS Secretariat), operating under Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Established to represent Nigeria at ECOWAS, coordinate regional integration and advance Nigerian interests in West Africa. Represents Nigeria at Economic Community of West African States. Participates in ECOWAS decision-making and governance. Promotes regional integration and free movement. Coordinates ECOWAS Common External Tariff implementation. Supports ECOWAS single currency (ECO) initiatives. Promotes peace and security in West Africa. Participates in ECOWAS peacekeeping and conflict resolution. Supports democracy and good governance initiatives. Coordinates on counter-terrorism and regional security. Promotes trade and economic cooperation. Addresses challenges from border closure disputes (Nigeria-Benin border closed 2019-2022), regional tensions and coordination gaps. Nigeria funds ~70% of ECOWAS budget but benefits questioned. Regional integration progress slow. Critical for regional diplomacy, ECOWAS coordination and advancing Nigerian leadership in West Africa despite integration challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To represent Nigeria at ECOWAS, promote regional integration, coordinate West African cooperation and advance Nigerian interests in regional bloc.

(b) Participate in ECOWAS decision-making, councils and commissions
(c) Promote regional integration and West African unity
(d) Support ECOWAS free movement protocol implementation
(e) Coordinate ECOWAS Common External Tariff (CET) policies
(f) Support ECOWAS single currency (ECO) development
(g) Promote peace and security in West Africa

(n) Advance Nigerian interests in ECOWAS decisions
(o) Address Nigeria funding ~70% of ECOWAS budget
(p) Navigate border closure disputes (Nigeria-Benin 2019-2022)
(q) Address regional tensions and member state conflicts
(r) Promote implementation of ECOWAS protocols
(s) Support regional infrastructure connectivity
(t) Advance regional integration and Nigerian leadership in ECOWAS despite slow integration progress and border disputes

## 764. NIGERIAN TELEVISION AUTHORITY (NTA)

**MDA Code:** 123003001

Nigerian Television Authority is federal government-owned television network broadcasting nationwide. Operating under Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. Established 1977 as Nigeria's official television broadcaster with stations across all states. Operates television stations in all 36 states and FCT. Provides news broadcasting and current affairs programming. Broadcasts government information and official communications. Produces Nigerian content including dramas, documentaries and entertainment. Operates NTA News covering national events. Provides educational programming. Broadcasts sports and cultural programs. Serves as official broadcaster for state functions. Operates NTA International reaching Nigerian diaspora. Faces challenges from private broadcasters competition, digital broadcasting transition, outdated equipment, funding constraints and declining viewership. Satellite TV (DSTV, StarTimes) and streaming services eroding viewership. State control raises editorial independence concerns. Equipment outdated requiring digital migration. Critical for public broadcasting, government communication and national television presence despite competition and financial challenges.

(b) Operate television stations in all 36 states and FCT
(c) Broadcast news and current affairs programming
(d) Disseminate government information and official communications
(e) Cover Presidential speeches and state functions
(f) Produce Nigerian content including dramas and series
(g) Create documentaries on Nigerian culture and heritage
(h) Provide educational television programming
(i) Broadcast sports events and coverage
(j) Promote Nigerian cultural programs and entertainment
(k) Operate NTA News as national news service
(l) Broadcast in English and Nigerian languages
(m) Operate NTA International for diaspora audiences
(n) Implement digital broadcasting transition

(p) Compete with satellite TV (DSTV, StarTimes)
(q) Address funding constraints and revenue challenges
(r) Upgrade outdated broadcasting equipment
(s) Navigate editorial independence under state ownership
(t) Advance public broadcasting and national television despite private broadcaster competition, declining viewership and funding challenges

## 702. NEWS AGENCY OF NIGERIA (NAN)

News Agency of Nigeria is federal news wire service providing news gathering and distribution. Operating under Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. ## 599. NATIONAL BROADCASTING COMMISSION (NBC)

**MDA Code:** 123008001

National Broadcasting Commission is federal regulatory agency overseeing broadcasting industry and electronic media. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. Established by NBC Act to regulate, license and monitor radio and television broadcasting. Issues broadcasting licenses for radio and TV stations. Regulates broadcast content ensuring compliance with broadcast code. Monitors broadcast stations for regulatory compliance. Enforces broadcast standards and ethics. Addresses hate speech, incitement and inflammatory broadcasts. Regulates political broadcasts during elections. Enforces local content requirements promoting Nigerian programs. Issues sanctions including fines and license suspension for violations. Promotes diversity in broadcasting and media pluralism. Regulates advertising content and commercial broadcasting. Manages broadcast spectrum allocation. Addresses challenges from digital broadcasting transition, fake news, regulatory independence concerns and constitutional free speech debates. Has sanctioned stations for broadcast code violations generating controversies. Critical for broadcast industry regulation, content standards and balancing press freedom with responsible broadcasting.

## 727. NIGERIA PRESS COUNCIL

**MDA Code:** 123009001

Nigeria Press Council is federal regulatory body overseeing print media and journalism ethics. Operating under Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. Established to regulate journalism, promote press freedom and enforce media ethics. Regulates print media - newspapers, magazines and online journalism. Promotes ethical journalism and professional standards. Addresses press misconduct and unethical reporting. Enforces Nigerian Press Code of Ethics. Mediates complaints between public and media. Promotes press freedom and freedom of expression. Protects journalists from harassment and intimidation. Accredits journalists and issues press identification cards. Addresses fake news and misinformation in media. Promotes factual, accurate reporting. Sanctions newspapers for ethical violations. Balances press freedom with responsibility. Collaborates with Nigerian Guild of Editors and Newspaper Proprietors Association. Faces challenges from digital media disruption, declining newspaper circulation, fake news proliferation and balancing freedom with regulation. #EndSARS protests highlighted role of social media vs. traditional journalism. Critical for journalism ethics, press freedom protection and ensuring responsible media despite digital transformation challenges.

(j) Promote factual, accurate and balanced reporting
(k) Sanction newspapers and journalists for ethical violations
(l) Balance press freedom with journalistic responsibility
(m) Promote investigative journalism and public interest reporting
(n) Address hate speech and inflammatory journalism
(o) Promote media literacy and public understanding
(p) Collaborate with Nigerian Guild of Editors
(q) Work with Newspaper Proprietors Association of Nigeria
(r) Address digital media disruption and online journalism challenges
(s) Combat fake news proliferation on social media
(t) Advance journalism ethics and press freedom ensuring responsible reporting balancing freedom with accountability despite digital transformation and fake news challenges

National Institute for Cultural Orientation is federal agency promoting national unity, cultural integration and civic values. Operating under Federal Ministry of Information and Culture. Established to promote national consciousness, cultural integration and peaceful coexistence among Nigeria's diverse ethnic groups. Conducts civic education promoting national values. Conducts research on cultural diversity and integration. Celebrates Nigeria's ethnic diversity constructively. Promotes inter-ethnic and inter-religious dialogue. Supports conflict resolution through cultural understanding. Conducts programs in schools promoting national values. Addresses challenges from ethnic tensions, religious conflicts and identity politics fragmenting Nigeria. Boko Haram, farmer-herder conflicts, IPOB separatism and other tensions threaten national cohesion. ## 711. NIGERIA CORRECTIONAL SERVICE (NCS)

**MDA Code:** 124002001

Nigeria Correctional Service is federal paramilitary agency managing prisons, correctional facilities and inmate rehabilitation. Operating under Federal Ministry of Interior. Formerly Nigerian Prisons Service renamed 2019 emphasizing corrections over punishment. Operates federal prisons and correctional facilities nationwide. Detains convicted criminals and awaiting trial inmates. Provides rehabilitation and reformation programs for inmates. Operates skills acquisition training for prisoners. Provides vocational education and literacy programs. Ensures humane treatment and prisoners' rights. Operates maximum security prisons for dangerous criminals. Manages remand centers for awaiting trial detainees. Addresses prison congestion - facilities overcrowded at 200-300% capacity. Awaiting trial detainees constitute 70%+ of prison population. Provides healthcare services for inmates. Ensures prison security preventing escapes. Collaborates with judiciary on speedy trials. Faces massive challenges - overcrowding, inhumane conditions, inadequate funding, awaiting trial backlog, insufficient rehabilitation programs. Prison reforms urgent - conditions criticized by rights groups. Critical for criminal justice, inmate rehabilitation and detention management despite severe overcrowding and systemic challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To manage correctional facilities, detain criminals, rehabilitate inmates, ensure humane treatment, address overcrowding and support criminal justice system.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Operate federal prisons and correctional facilities nationwide
(b) Detain convicted criminals serving prison sentences
(c) Hold awaiting trial detainees pending court hearings
(d) Provide rehabilitation and reformation programs for inmates
(e) Operate skills acquisition training for prisoners
(f) Provide vocational education and literacy programs
(g) Ensure humane treatment and respect prisoners' rights
(h) Operate maximum security prisons for dangerous criminals
(i) Manage remand centers for pre-trial detainees
(j) Address severe prison congestion (200-300% capacity)
(k) Reduce awaiting trial detainees (70%+ of inmates)
(l) Provide healthcare services for inmates
(m) Ensure prison security and prevent escapes
(n) Manage convicted inmates' sentences and release
(o) Operate community service programs as alternatives
(p) Provide psychological counseling for inmates
(q) Collaborate with judiciary on speedy trials reducing backlog
(r) Promote prison reforms and corrections focus
(s) Address inhumane conditions criticized by rights organizations
(t) Advance criminal justice and inmate rehabilitation managing correctional facilities despite severe overcrowding, awaiting trial backlog and urgent need for reforms

## 716. NIGERIA IMMIGRATION SERVICE (NIS)

**MDA Code:** 124003001

Nigeria Immigration Service is federal paramilitary agency controlling borders, managing immigration and issuing passports. Operating under Federal Ministry of Interior. Established to regulate entry/exit, prevent illegal immigration and control Nigerian borders. Controls Nigerian borders at land, air and sea entry points. Issues Nigerian passports (standard and e-passport). Processes visa applications for foreigners visiting Nigeria. Prevents illegal immigration and border infiltration. Combats human trafficking at borders. Monitors foreigners resident in Nigeria. Issues residence permits and work permits. Conducts deportation of illegal immigrants. Operates border patrol preventing smuggling. Collaborates with customs on border security. Addresses porous borders enabling illegal entry. Biometric enrollment for passport issuance. Operates passport offices nationwide. Faces challenges from porous land borders (Benin, Niger, Chad, Cameroon), corruption allegations, passport processing delays, limited border infrastructure and irregular migration. Nigeria has extensive borders difficult to patrol. Critical for border security, immigration control, passport services and preventing illegal immigration despite porous borders and capacity constraints.

**Core Mandate:** To control Nigerian borders, regulate immigration, issue passports, prevent illegal entry, manage foreigners and ensure border security.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Control Nigerian borders at land, air and sea entry points
(b) Issue Nigerian passports (standard and biometric e-passport)
(c) Process visa applications for foreigners visiting Nigeria
(d) Prevent illegal immigration and unauthorized border crossing
(e) Combat human trafficking at border checkpoints
(f) Monitor foreigners resident in Nigeria
(g) Issue residence permits and expatriate quotas
(h) Process work permits for foreign workers
(i) Conduct deportation of illegal immigrants and overstayers
(j) Operate border patrol preventing smuggling and infiltration
(k) Collaborate with customs on integrated border management
(l) Address porous land borders with Benin, Niger, Chad, Cameroon
(m) Conduct biometric enrollment for passport applicants
(n) Operate passport offices in states and abroad
(o) Process passport renewals and replacements
(p) Verify travelers' documents at airports and ports
(q) Maintain immigration database and records
(r) Address passport processing delays and backlogs
(s) Combat corruption in immigration services
(t) Advance border security and immigration control managing entry/exit, issuing passports and preventing illegal immigration despite porous borders and capacity challenges

## 728. NIGERIA SECURITY AND CIVIL DEFENCE CORPS (NSCDC)

**MDA Code:** 124004001

Nigeria Security and Civil Defence Corps is federal paramilitary agency providing security for critical infrastructure and civil protection. Operating under Federal Ministry of Interior. Established to protect government facilities, critical national assets and provide civil defense. Protects critical national infrastructure - oil pipelines, power installations, government buildings. Combats pipeline vandalism and oil theft. Provides security for INEC during elections. Guards government offices and federal facilities. Protects schools and educational institutions. Provides disaster response and civil protection. Conducts search and rescue operations. Addresses security threats to national assets. Operates in all states and FCT. Provides security at events and public gatherings. Collaborates with police, military and security agencies. Faces challenges from inadequate equipment, overlapping mandates with police, limited training and capacity constraints. Petroleum products pipeline vandalism costs Nigeria billions annually. NSCDC role expanded beyond original civil defense to paramilitary security force. Critical for infrastructure security, civil protection and preventing pipeline vandalism despite capacity limitations.

**Core Mandate:** To protect critical infrastructure, combat pipeline vandalism, provide civil defense, secure government facilities and safeguard national assets from sabotage.

(b) Guard oil pipelines preventing vandalism and theft
(c) Combat petroleum products pipeline vandalism costing billions
(d) Protect power installations and electricity infrastructure
(e) Guard government buildings and federal offices
(f) Provide security for INEC during elections
(g) Protect schools and educational institutions from attacks
(h) Provide disaster response and emergency management
(i) Conduct search and rescue operations during emergencies
(j) Provide civil protection and civil defense services
(k) Address security threats to national assets
(l) Operate commandos in all 36 states and FCT
(m) Provide security at events and public gatherings
(n) Investigate pipeline sabotage and infrastructure attacks
(o) Arrest pipeline vandals and oil thieves
(p) Collaborate with police on security operations
(q) Coordinate with military on critical infrastructure protection
(r) Address overlapping mandates with Nigeria Police
(s) Build capacity through training and equipment provision
(t) Advance infrastructure security and civil protection combating pipeline vandalism preventing oil theft despite capacity constraints and overlapping security agency mandates

## 710. NIGERIA COMMUNICATIONS SATELLITE (NigComSat)

**MDA Code:** 156003001

Nigeria Communications Satellite Limited is federal agency operating communications satellites for telecommunications and broadcasting. Operating under Federal Ministry of Communications. Established to provide satellite communications infrastructure and broadcast services. Operates NigComSat satellites providing communications capacity. Provides satellite capacity for telecommunications companies. Supports broadcasting via satellite transmissions. Offers internet connectivity through satellite. Provides VSAT services for remote areas. Supports government communications infrastructure. Offers satellite bandwidth for commercial clients. Addresses digital divide through satellite connectivity. Provides disaster recovery communications backup. Supports border surveillance and security communications. Collaborates with NASRDA on satellite operations. Nigeria launched first communication satellite NigComSat-1 (2007) which failed 2008. NigComSat-1R launched 2011 as replacement. ## 767. NIGERIAN POSTAL SERVICE (NIPOST)

**MDA Code:** 156006001

Nigerian Postal Service is federal agency providing postal and courier services nationwide. Operating under Federal Ministry of Communications and Digital Economy. Established to provide mail delivery, postal services and support e-commerce logistics. Provides mail and parcel delivery services nationwide. Operates post offices across Nigeria. Issues postage stamps and postal products. Provides courier and express delivery services. Supports e-commerce and online shopping logistics. Provides postal money orders and financial services. Operates postal savings and microfinance. Issues stamp duties and revenue stamps. Provides government document delivery. Collaborates with international postal unions (UPU). Faces existential challenges from digital communication replacing mail, courier companies (DHL, FedEx) competition, e-commerce logistics competitors, declining mail volumes and financial struggles. Email, WhatsApp replacing letters. Postal services globally struggling with digitalization. NIPOST requires transformation to remain relevant. Critical for last-mile delivery, financial inclusion and postal services despite digital disruption threatening traditional postal model.

(p) Address e-commerce logistics competitors (Jumia Logistics, GIG)
(q) Tackle declining mail volumes from email adoption
(r) Address financial struggles and revenue challenges
(s) Transform postal services for digital economy

## 682. OFFICE OF THE NATIONAL SECURITY ADVISER (ONSA)

**MDA Code:** 157001001

Office of the National Security Adviser is apex national security coordination body advising President on security matters. Located in Abuja, operating directly under Presidency. National Security Adviser appointed by President coordinates Nigeria's security architecture. Advises President on national security policy and strategy. Coordinates security agencies (military, police, DSS, NIA, NSCDC). Develops national security strategy addressing terrorism, insurgency and threats. Coordinates counter-terrorism operations against Boko Haram and ISWAP. Oversees National Counter Terrorism Centre operations. Manages Multinational Joint Task Force (MNJTF) coordination. Coordinates intelligence sharing among security agencies. Addresses security challenges - Boko Haram insurgency (35,000+ deaths), northwestern banditry, kidnapping epidemic, farmer-herder conflicts, IPOB agitations, oil theft. Coordinates military operations in northeast, northwest and other theaters. Oversees defense procurement and security equipment acquisition. Manages security budgets and funding allocation. Collaborates with international security partners. Current NSA coordinates whole-of-government security response. Faces challenges from multiple simultaneous security crises, inter-agency coordination difficulties and resource constraints. Critical for national security coordination, strategic planning and managing Nigeria's complex multi-dimensional security threats.

**Core Mandate:** To advise President on national security, coordinate security agencies, develop security strategy, manage counter-terrorism operations and ensure effective national security response.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Advise President on national security policy and strategy
(b) Coordinate Nigeria's security agencies and architecture
(c) Coordinate military, police, DSS, NIA and other security services
(d) Develop national security strategy addressing threats
(e) Coordinate counter-terrorism operations against Boko Haram and ISWAP
(f) Oversee National Counter Terrorism Centre (NCTC) operations
(g) Manage Multinational Joint Task Force (MNJTF) against Boko Haram
(h) Coordinate intelligence sharing among security agencies
(i) Address Boko Haram insurgency (35,000+ deaths since 2009)
(j) Coordinate response to northwestern banditry and kidnapping
(k) Address farmer-herder conflicts and communal violence
(l) Manage IPOB separatist agitations and southeastern security
(m) Coordinate military operations across security theaters
(n) Oversee defense procurement and security equipment acquisition
(o) Manage security budgets and resource allocation
(p) Coordinate border security with neighboring countries
(q) Address oil theft and pipeline vandalism in Niger Delta
(r) Collaborate with US, UK, France and international security partners
(s) Promote inter-agency cooperation and information sharing
(t) Advance national security through strategic coordination managing multiple simultaneous crises (insurgency, banditry, kidnapping) ensuring coherent whole-of-government security response

National Institute for Security Studies is premier security training and research institution for security professionals and policymakers. Operating under Office of National Security Adviser. Conducts security research on Nigeria's security challenges. Studies terrorism, insurgency and violent extremism. Researches cyber security and emerging security threats. Promotes inter-agency security cooperation and coordination. Trains civilian security officials promoting civil-military relations. Collaborates with international security institutions. Addresses Nigeria's complex security environment - Boko Haram, banditry, kidnapping, separatist movements, farmer-herder conflicts. Critical for strategic security leadership, professional security management and coordinating Nigeria's multi-dimensional security response.

**Statutory Functions:**

(b) Conduct Executive Intelligence Management Course for intelligence chiefs

## 646. NATIONAL INTELLIGENCE AGENCY (NIA)

**MDA Code:** 157003001

National Intelligence Agency is Nigeria's external intelligence service conducting foreign intelligence operations. Operating under Office of National Security Adviser. Established to gather foreign intelligence, conduct counterintelligence and protect Nigeria's external security interests. Conducts foreign intelligence collection on threats to national security. Monitors international terrorism and extremist groups. Gathers intelligence on foreign governments and organizations affecting Nigerian interests. Conducts counterintelligence preventing foreign espionage in Nigeria. Protects Nigerian diplomatic missions abroad. Conducts intelligence assessments and analysis for government. Monitors regional security threats from neighboring countries. Gathers intelligence on arms trafficking, drug trafficking and transnational crimes. Supports foreign policy formulation with intelligence. Conducts covert operations protecting national interests. Collaborates with foreign intelligence agencies (CIA, MI6, DGSE, etc.). Operates under Official Secrets Act maintaining secrecy. Faces challenges from resource constraints, sophisticated adversaries and coordination with other intelligence agencies. Intelligence failures contributed to security challenges including Boko Haram rise. Critical for national security, foreign intelligence and protecting Nigeria's external interests through intelligence operations.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct foreign intelligence operations, gather strategic intelligence, prevent foreign espionage, support foreign policy and protect Nigeria's external security interests.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct foreign intelligence collection on national security threats
(b) Monitor international terrorism and extremist organizations
(c) Gather intelligence on foreign governments affecting Nigerian interests
(d) Conduct strategic intelligence analysis for government decision-making
(e) Prevent foreign espionage and intelligence operations in Nigeria
(f) Protect Nigerian diplomatic missions and embassies abroad
(g) Conduct intelligence on regional security threats
(h) Monitor neighboring countries' activities affecting Nigeria
(i) Gather intelligence on arms trafficking and weapons proliferation
(j) Monitor international drug trafficking networks
(k) Track transnational organized crime threatening Nigeria
(l) Support foreign policy formulation with intelligence assessments
(m) Conduct covert operations protecting national interests
(n) Monitor diaspora communities for security threats
(o) Gather intelligence on cyber threats from foreign actors
(p) Conduct counterintelligence protecting classified information
(q) Collaborate with foreign intelligence agencies (CIA, MI6, Mossad, etc.)
(r) Share intelligence with regional intelligence services
(s) Operate under Official Secrets Act maintaining operational security
(t) Advance national security through foreign intelligence protecting Nigeria's external interests despite resource constraints and sophisticated adversaries

## 647. NATIONAL INTELLIGENCE AGENCY PENSION BOARD

**MDA Code:** 157003002

National Intelligence Agency Pension Board is specialized pension administration body managing retirement benefits for NIA personnel. Operating under National Intelligence Agency. Established to administer pensions for retired NIA operatives, intelligence officers and staff. Processes retirement benefits for NIA retirees. Calculates pension entitlements for intelligence officers. Pays monthly pensions to retired NIA personnel. Manages gratuities and lump sum retirement payments. Handles death benefits for families of deceased operatives. Maintains confidential database of NIA pensioners. Verifies pensioners through secure identification processes. Addresses pension arrears and outstanding payments. Provides special consideration for covert operatives' retirement. Operates with security protocols protecting intelligence personnel identities. Collaborates with NIA administration on retirement processing. Addresses challenges from classified nature of intelligence work, identity protection requirements and limited public information. Intelligence personnel retirement requires special handling maintaining operational security even post-retirement. Critical for intelligence community welfare, retirement security and maintaining morale among serving intelligence officers knowing retirement is secured.

**Core Mandate:** To administer pensions for retired NIA personnel, manage intelligence officers' retirement benefits, maintain security protocols and ensure welfare of retired intelligence operatives.

(b) Process retirement benefits for NIA operatives
(c) Calculate pension entitlements based on rank and service years

(e) Manage gratuities and lump sum retirement payments

(g) Maintain confidential database of NIA pensioners
(h) Verify pensioners through secure identification processes
(i) Address pension arrears and outstanding payments
(j) Provide special consideration for covert operatives' retirement
(k) Protect identities of intelligence personnel in retirement records
(l) Operate with security protocols maintaining operational security
(m) Handle disability pensions for officers injured in service
(n) Process early retirement for intelligence personnel
(o) Manage pension fund investments for NIA retirees
(p) Collaborate with NIA administration on retirement processing
(q) Maintain classified pension records with appropriate security clearances
(r) Ensure continuation of security protocols post-retirement
(s) Address unique pension challenges from classified intelligence work
(t) Advance intelligence community welfare ensuring retirement security for operatives maintaining morale among serving officers through secure pension administration
