## 858. UNIVERSAL BASIC EDUCATION COMMISSION (UBEC)

**MDA Code:** 517003001

Universal Basic Education Commission coordinates implementation of Universal Basic Education program. Headquartered in Abuja. Operates under Federal Ministry of Education. Established under UBE Act 2004. Provides 2% Consolidated Revenue Fund intervention for basic education. Matches state government contributions. Supports primary and junior secondary education infrastructure and quality.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate Universal Basic Education, provide federal intervention funds, support basic education infrastructure and ensure quality basic education nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate Universal Basic Education program implementation
(b) Provide 2% Consolidated Revenue Fund intervention
(c) Match state government basic education contributions
(d) Support primary school infrastructure development
(e) Support junior secondary school infrastructure
(f) Build and renovate classrooms nationwide
(g) Provide instructional materials to schools
(h) Support teacher training and development
(i) Promote girl-child education enrollment
(j) Address out-of-school children challenges
(k) Support inclusive education for disabled children
(l) Provide school furniture and equipment
(m) Support adult literacy programs
(n) Monitor basic education quality standards
(o) Collaborate with State Universal Basic Education Boards
(p) Ensure matching grants utilization
(q) Conduct basic education assessments
(r) Support early childhood education development
(s) Promote basic education access and enrollment
(t) Advance basic education quality through federal intervention funds supporting infrastructure and teacher development nationwide

West African Examinations Council International coordinates WAEC operations across West African member countries. Headquartered in Accra, Ghana with Nigerian national office. Operates as regional examination body. Member countries include Nigeria, Ghana, Sierra Leone, Liberia and Gambia. Coordinates regional examination standards. Manages international WAEC operations.

(t) Advance regional educational assessment through coordinated examination standards across West African member countries

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 641-660

## 641. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR EDUCATIONAL PLANNING AND ADMINISTRATION (NIEPA)

**MDA Code:** 517007001

National Institute for Educational Planning and Administration is federal institution providing training, research and capacity building in education planning and management. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to train education planners, administrators and managers supporting evidence-based education policy. Provides professional training for education administrators including principals, headmasters and supervisors. Conducts capacity building for education ministry officials and planners. Offers programs in educational planning, management and policy analysis. Trains on education data management and statistics. Provides school management and leadership training. Conducts research on education planning and administration. Develops education management information systems. Trains on education budgeting and resource allocation. Promotes effective school administration. Provides continuing professional development for education managers. Collaborates with universities offering educational administration programs. Addresses challenges from weak education planning capacity, poor resource management in schools and limited data-driven planning. Nigeria's education system faces management challenges - teacher shortages, infrastructure gaps, poor learning outcomes. Critical for education quality improvement through professional management, effective planning and data-driven decision-making.

**Core Mandate:** To train education planners and administrators, build management capacity, conduct education planning research and support professional education management and policy development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Train education planners and administrators for effective school management
(b) Provide professional development for principals and headmasters
(c) Conduct capacity building for education ministry officials
(d) Offer programs in educational planning and policy analysis
(e) Train on education data management and statistics
(f) Provide school management and leadership training
(g) Conduct research on education planning and administration
(h) Develop education management information systems (EMIS)
(i) Train on education budgeting and financial management
(j) Promote effective school administration and governance
(k) Provide supervisory training for education inspectors
(l) Train on curriculum planning and implementation
(m) Conduct strategic planning for education development
(n) Provide continuing professional development for education managers
(o) Research education quality improvement strategies
(p) Train on teacher management and deployment
(q) Develop education planning tools and methodologies
(r) Collaborate with universities on educational administration programs
(s) Promote data-driven education planning and decision-making
(t) Advance education quality through professional management building capacity for planning, administration and policy development supporting Nigeria's education system improvement

## 650. NATIONAL LIBRARY OF NIGERIA

**MDA Code:** 517008001

National Library of Nigeria is federal institution serving as repository of Nigerian published materials and national bibliographic center. Headquartered in Abuja with branches nationwide, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to collect, preserve and provide access to Nigeria's documentary heritage. Serves as legal deposit library receiving copies of all Nigerian publications. Maintains comprehensive collection of Nigerian books, journals and publications. Preserves Nigeria's documentary heritage and intellectual output. Operates National Bibliographic Center creating national bibliography. Provides library and information services to researchers and public. Maintains special collections including rare books and manuscripts. Digitizes Nigerian publications for preservation and access. Provides reference and research support services. Promotes reading culture and library use. Operates branch libraries across Nigeria. Collaborates with state and university libraries. Provides professional development for librarians. Addresses challenges from inadequate funding, limited acquisitions, infrastructure constraints and digitization needs. Nigerian publishing industry requires robust national library. Many Nigerian publications not preserved or accessible. Critical for knowledge preservation, research support and ensuring Nigerian intellectual heritage is collected and accessible to future generations.

**Core Mandate:** To collect and preserve Nigerian publications, serve as legal deposit library, maintain national bibliography, provide library services and preserve Nigeria's documentary heritage.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Serve as legal deposit library for all Nigerian publications
(b) Collect copies of books, journals and publications published in Nigeria
(c) Preserve Nigeria's documentary heritage and intellectual output
(d) Maintain comprehensive collection of Nigerian published materials
(e) Operate National Bibliographic Center creating national bibliography
(f) Develop and maintain Nigerian bibliographic database
(g) Provide library and information services to researchers and public
(h) Maintain special collections including rare books and manuscripts
(i) Preserve historical Nigerian documents and archives
(j) Digitize Nigerian publications for preservation and accessibility
(k) Provide online access to digital collections
(l) Offer reference and research support services
(m) Promote reading culture and library utilization
(n) Operate branch libraries across Nigerian states
(o) Provide interlibrary loan services
(p) Collaborate with state libraries and university libraries
(q) Provide professional development for Nigerian librarians
(r) Support Nigerian scholarship and research through collections
(s) Promote ISBN registration for Nigerian publishers
(t) Advance knowledge preservation and research support ensuring Nigerian publications, intellectual heritage and documentary materials are collected, preserved and accessible supporting scholarship and future generations

## 624. NATIONAL EXAMINATIONS COUNCIL (NECO)

**MDA Code:** 517009001

National Examinations Council is federal examination body conducting school certificate and entrance examinations. Headquartered in Minna, Niger State, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established in 1999 to diversify examination administration and provide alternative to WAEC. Conducts Senior School Certificate Examination (SSCE) for SS3 students. Conducts Junior School Certificate Examination (JSCE) for JSS3 students. Administers Common Entrance Examination for admission to Federal Unity Schools. Conducts BECE (Basic Education Certificate Examination). Provides Nigerian certification alternative to WAEC (established 1952). Issues certificates accepted for tertiary admissions and employment. Sets examination standards and syllabi. Conducts examinations in public and private schools nationwide. Promotes examination integrity and combats malpractice. Registers millions of candidates annually for SSCE. Collaborates with schools, universities and JAMB. Faces challenges from examination malpractice, result delays, logistics difficulties and recognition disputes with some institutions. Provides competition to WAEC improving examination services. Critical for secondary education certification and tertiary institution admission requirements.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct school certificate examinations, issue certificates, ensure examination integrity, provide alternative certification to WAEC and support Nigeria's education assessment system.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct Senior School Certificate Examination (SSCE) for SS3 students
(b) Administer Junior School Certificate Examination (JSCE) for JSS3
(c) Conduct Common Entrance Examination for Federal Unity Schools admission
(d) Administer Basic Education Certificate Examination (BECE)
(e) Register millions of candidates annually for examinations
(f) Set examination standards and syllabi for subjects
(g) Develop examination questions testing curriculum coverage
(h) Conduct examinations in public and private schools nationwide
(9) Mark and grade examination scripts
(j) Issue certificates to successful candidates
(k) Provide Nigerian alternative to WAEC certification
(l) Ensure examination security and integrity
(m) Combat examination malpractice and cheating
(n) Prosecute examination fraud and certificate forgery
(o) Process examination results and release promptly
(p) Collaborate with schools on examination administration
(q) Coordinate with JAMB on tertiary admission requirements
(r) Promote fair and credible examinations
(s) Address logistics challenges in remote areas
(t) Advance education assessment and certification providing credible SSCE certificates for millions supporting tertiary admission and employment

## 611. NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR NOMADIC EDUCATION (NCNE)

**MDA Code:** 517011001

National Commission for Nomadic Education is federal agency providing education for nomadic populations including pastoralists, migrant fishermen and migrant farmers. Headquartered in Kaduna, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to address educational marginalization of mobile communities. Provides education for nomadic Fulani pastoralists moving with cattle. Operates mobile schools following pastoralist movements. Provides education for migrant fishermen along rivers and coasts. Educates children of migrant farmers in shifting cultivation areas. Develops nomadic education curriculum adapted to mobile lifestyles. Operates boarding schools for nomadic children. Promotes girls' education among pastoralist communities. Trains teachers for nomadic education. Provides adult literacy for nomadic adults. Collaborates with states on nomadic education programs. Addresses challenges from mobility, cultural resistance to Western education and insecurity. Nigeria has estimated 9-12 million nomadic people, mainly Fulani pastoralists. Pastoralist children often lack access to formal education. Farmer-herder conflicts and insecurity disrupt nomadic education. Critical for inclusive education, reducing illiteracy and integrating nomadic populations.

**Core Mandate:** To provide education for nomadic populations, operate mobile schools, promote pastoralist education, address educational marginalization and ensure nomadic children access quality education.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide education for nomadic populations including pastoralists
(b) Educate Fulani pastoralist children following cattle herds
(c) Operate mobile schools adapting to pastoralist movements
(d) Provide education for migrant fishermen along rivers and coasts
(e) Educate children of migrant farmers
(f) Develop nomadic education curriculum adapted to mobile lifestyles
(g) Operate boarding schools for nomadic children
(h) Promote girls' education among pastoralist communities
(i) Address cultural barriers to educating nomadic girls
(j) Train teachers specialized in nomadic education
(k) Provide teaching materials for mobile schools
(l) Conduct adult literacy programs for nomadic adults
(m) Promote awareness of education importance among nomads
(n) Collaborate with states on nomadic education implementation
(o) Address pastoralist mobility challenges to education delivery
(p) Integrate nomadic education with formal education system
(q) Address farmer-herder conflicts affecting nomadic education
(r) Operate schools in insurgency-affected pastoral areas
(s) Promote nomadic children's access to secondary education
(t) Advance inclusive education for 9-12 million nomadic Nigerians reducing educational marginalization and illiteracy among pastoralist communities despite mobility and security challenges

## 621. NATIONAL EDUCATION RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT COUNCIL (NERDC)

**MDA Code:** 517012001

National Education Research and Development Council is federal agency conducting educational research and developing school curricula. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to develop curriculum, conduct education research and support evidence-based education policy. Develops national curriculum for primary and secondary schools. Designs subject syllabi and learning materials. Conducts educational research on teaching methods and learning outcomes. Develops curriculum frameworks and standards. Researches education quality and student achievement. Provides curriculum implementation support for schools. Develops teacher training materials. Conducts research on literacy, numeracy and basic education. Studies education innovations and pedagogy. Collaborates with universities and education faculties. Addresses curriculum relevance, quality and effectiveness. Provides evidence for education policy reforms. Addresses challenges from limited research funding, implementation gaps between curriculum and practice, and need for curriculum updates reflecting 21st century skills. Critical for education quality, curriculum development and ensuring Nigerian education system produces desired learning outcomes through research-informed curriculum and pedagogy.

**Core Mandate:** To develop national curriculum, conduct education research, design learning materials, support curriculum implementation and provide evidence for education policy and quality improvement.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Develop national curriculum for primary and secondary education
(b) Design subject syllabi and curriculum frameworks
(c) Develop learning materials and educational resources
(d) Conduct educational research on teaching and learning
(e) Research education quality and student achievement
(f) Study literacy and numeracy outcomes
(g) Research effective pedagogy and teaching methods
(h) Develop curriculum standards and benchmarks
(i) Provide curriculum implementation support for schools
(j) Develop teacher training and professional development materials
(k) Research education innovations and best practices
(l) Study curriculum relevance to labor market needs
(m) Conduct research on basic education effectiveness
(n) Research inclusive education and special needs
(o) Study ICT integration in education
(p) Provide evidence for education policy reforms
(q) Collaborate with universities and education faculties
(r) Engage international education research networks
(s) Promote 21st century skills in curriculum
(t) Advance education quality and curriculum development through research ensuring Nigerian curriculum produces desired learning outcomes and prepares students for modern workforce

## 601. NATIONAL BUSINESS AND TECHNICAL EDUCATION BOARD (NABTEB)

**MDA Code:** 517013001

National Business and Technical Education Board is federal examination body conducting technical and business education examinations. Headquartered in Benin City, Edo State, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to conduct examinations for students in technical colleges, business schools and vocational training centers. Conducts National Business Certificate (NBC) examinations for business education. Conducts National Technical Certificate (NTC) examinations for technical trades. Administers Advanced National Business Certificate (ANBC) examinations. Conducts Advanced National Technical Certificate (ANTC) examinations. Sets syllabi for business and technical education. Prepares examination questions testing practical and theoretical knowledge. Conducts practical examinations in workshops and laboratories. Issues certificates to successful candidates. Provides alternative certification pathway to WAEC/NECO. Promotes technical and vocational education and training. Collaborates with NBTE, technical colleges and vocational centers. Addresses challenges from limited examination centers, delayed results and recognition issues. Complements academic education providing certification for technical and business skills essential for employment and entrepreneurship.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct business and technical education examinations, certify vocational skills, promote TVET and provide certification for technical college and vocational training graduates.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct National Business Certificate (NBC) examinations
(b) Conduct National Technical Certificate (NTC) examinations for trades
(c) Administer Advanced National Business Certificate (ANBC) examinations
(d) Conduct Advanced National Technical Certificate (ANTC) examinations
(e) Set syllabi for business and technical education courses
(f) Prepare examination questions testing practical and theoretical skills
(g) Conduct practical examinations in workshops and laboratories
(h) Assess vocational competencies and trade skills
(i) Issue certificates to successful candidates
(j) Provide alternative certification pathway to academic examinations
(k) Promote technical and vocational education
(l) Collaborate with NBTE on technical education standards
(m) Coordinate with technical colleges on examination administration
(n) Develop examination standards for various trades
(o) Conduct examinations in carpentry, welding, electrical work and other trades
(p) Assess business education skills in accounting, secretarial studies and commerce
(q) Operate examination centers in technical institutions
(r) Ensure examination integrity and quality
(s) Address recognition and equivalency with other certificates
(t) Advance TVET certification providing recognized credentials for technical skills supporting employment and entrepreneurship in trades and business

**MDA Code:** 517014001

Teachers Registration Council of Nigeria is federal regulatory body for teaching profession. Operates under Federal Ministry of Education. Registers professional teachers nationwide. Licenses practicing teachers. Accredits teacher education programs. Sets teaching professional standards.

## 609. NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR COLLEGES OF EDUCATION (NCCE)

**MDA Code:** 517016001

National Commission for Colleges of Education is federal regulatory body overseeing colleges of education and teacher training. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to regulate teacher education, accredit college programs and ensure quality teacher training. Accredits academic programs in Federal, State and Private Colleges of Education. Develops minimum academic standards for teacher education. Conducts accreditation visits to colleges inspecting facilities and programs. Regulates Nigeria Certificate in Education (NCE) - minimum teaching qualification. Develops curriculum for teacher training programs. Sets standards for teaching practice and practicum. Regulates establishment of colleges of education. Approves new academic programs in teacher education. Monitors quality assurance in teacher training. Collaborates with universities and TRCN on teacher development. Addresses challenges from inadequate facilities, poor funding and teacher quality concerns. NCE is minimum qualification for teaching in Nigerian schools. Quality teachers critical for education system effectiveness. Faces challenges from declining interest in teaching profession and limited practical training.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate colleges of education, accredit teacher training programs, ensure quality teacher education and maintain standards for Nigeria Certificate in Education.

(b) Accredit academic programs in colleges of education

(g) Set standards for teaching practice and classroom practicum

(t) Advance teacher education quality ensuring Nigeria's teachers receive proper training through NCE programs supporting effective teaching and learning

## 691. NATIONAL TEACHERS INSTITUTE (NTI)

**MDA Code:** 517017001

National Teachers Institute is federal distance learning institution providing teacher education and professional development. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to upgrade unqualified teachers, provide in-service training and improve teaching quality through distance education. Provides Nigeria Certificate in Education (NCE) through distance learning. Upgrades unqualified teachers to NCE qualification. Offers Pivotal Teacher Training Programme (PTTP). Conducts Teachers' Grade II to NCE upgrading. Provides in-service professional development for teachers. Operates Professional Diploma in Education (PDE) programs. Trains teachers in pedagogy and subject content. Provides special education teacher training. Conducts ICT training for teachers. Operates study centers nationwide for teacher support. Collaborates with universities on degree programs for teachers. Addresses teacher quality crisis - many unqualified teachers, poor pedagogical skills, limited continuous professional development. Nigeria has ~1.6 million teachers, many require upgrading. NCE is minimum teaching qualification but implementation gaps. Faces challenges from quality concerns in distance education, limited practical training and teacher motivation. Critical for teacher quality improvement, professional development and upgrading teaching workforce.

**Core Mandate:** To provide distance teacher education, upgrade unqualified teachers, deliver professional development, improve teaching quality and support teacher workforce development.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide Nigeria Certificate in Education (NCE) through distance learning
(b) Upgrade unqualified teachers to NCE minimum qualification
(c) Conduct Pivotal Teacher Training Programme (PTTP)
(d) Offer Teachers' Grade II to NCE upgrading programs
(e) Provide in-service professional development for serving teachers
(f) Operate Professional Diploma in Education (PDE) programs
(g) Train teachers in pedagogy and teaching methods
(h) Provide subject content training for teachers
(i) Conduct special education teacher training
(j) Provide ICT skills training for teachers
(k) Train on classroom management and student assessment
(l) Operate study centers nationwide for teacher support
(m) Conduct facilitation and practical teaching sessions
(n) Collaborate with universities on B.Ed programs for teachers
(o) Address teacher quality crisis affecting learning outcomes
(p) Upgrade 1.6M+ teachers requiring professional development
(q) Implement NCE as minimum teaching qualification
(r) Provide continuing professional development programs
(s) Address quality concerns in distance teacher education
(t) Advance teaching quality through distance education upgrading unqualified teachers and providing professional development despite quality challenges

## 639. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR CONSTRUCTION TECHNOLOGY, UROMI

**MDA Code:** 517018024

National Institute for Construction Technology is specialized federal technical training institution for construction industry. Located in Uromi, Edo State, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to provide advanced construction technology training, develop construction skills and support building industry manpower. Provides diploma and certificate programs in building technology, civil engineering technology and construction management. Trains construction technologists and supervisors. Offers programs in quantity surveying and building economics. Provides training in construction materials and testing. Teaches construction project management and site supervision. Offers structural design and construction methods training. Provides training in building services (plumbing, electrical, HVAC). Conducts practical construction training using workshops and building sites. Trains in modern construction techniques and technologies. Supports infrastructure development through skilled workforce. Collaborates with construction companies on practical training. Addresses construction industry skills gaps and quality challenges. Nigeria's massive infrastructure deficit requires skilled construction professionals. Faces challenges from equipment needs, industry linkages and graduate employment. Critical for construction sector development, infrastructure delivery and producing skilled construction technologists.

**Core Mandate:** To provide construction technology education, train building technologists, develop construction skills and support infrastructure development through skilled construction workforce.

(b) Offer diploma programs in building technology
(c) Provide civil engineering technology training

(s) Promote construction quality and safety standards

## 694. NATIONAL UNIVERSITIES COMMISSION (NUC)

**MDA Code:** 517020001

National Universities Commission is apex federal regulatory body overseeing university education in Nigeria. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to regulate, accredit and ensure quality in Nigerian universities. Regulates Federal, State and Private universities (200+ universities total). Accredits university academic programs ensuring quality standards. Approves establishment of new universities. Conducts accreditation visits to universities assessing facilities, staffing and programs. Sets Minimum Academic Standards (MAS) for all programs. Regulates university admissions and student enrollment. Coordinates JAMB on university admission requirements. Addresses proliferation of universities - rapid growth from 15 universities (1985) to 200+ universities (2024). Monitors quality assurance in university education. Regulates staff-student ratios and lecturer qualifications. Addresses challenges from poor funding (universities underfunded), quality deterioration, "academic prostitution" (fake accreditation), proliferation of substandard private universities, ASUU strikes disrupting academic calendars. Coordinates university research and postgraduate programs. Critical for university education quality, maintaining standards and regulating Nigeria's massive higher education expansion.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate university education, accredit academic programs, approve new universities, ensure quality standards and oversee Nigeria's 200+ universities.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate Federal, State and Private universities (200+ total)
(b) Accredit university academic programs ensuring quality
(c) Approve establishment of new universities
(d) Conduct accreditation visits assessing facilities and programs
(e) Set Minimum Academic Standards (MAS) for all programs
(f) Regulate undergraduate and postgraduate programs
(g) Monitor staff-student ratios and lecturer qualifications
(h) Coordinate with JAMB on university admission requirements
(i) Address university proliferation and quality control
(j) Monitor quality assurance in university education
(k) Regulate university governance and administration
(l) Address poor funding affecting university quality
(m) Combat fake accreditation and degree mills
(n) Regulate private universities ensuring standards
(o) Coordinate university research programs
(p) Address ASUU strikes disrupting academic calendars
(q) Promote ICT integration in university education
(r) Monitor university infrastructure and facilities
(s) Ensure academic freedom and university autonomy
(t) Advance university education quality regulating 200+ universities ensuring academic standards despite funding constraints, proliferation challenges and quality deterioration

## 866. UNIVERSITY OF IBADAN (UI)

**MDA Code:** 517021001

University of Ibadan is Nigeria's premier federal university located in Ibadan, Oyo State. Operates under Federal Ministry of Education. Established 1948 as University College Ibadan. First university in Nigeria. Provides undergraduate and postgraduate programs across faculties. Operates medical school. Premier first generation university with strong academic reputation.

## 780. OBAFEMI AWOLOWO UNIVERSITY (OAU), ILE-IFE

**MDA Code:** 517021005

Obafemi Awolowo University is prestigious federal university providing comprehensive tertiary education. Located in Ile-Ife, Osun State, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established 1961 as University of Ife, renamed 1987 after Chief Obafemi Awolowo (Yoruba leader and statesman). Among Nigeria's oldest and most prestigious universities alongside University of Ibadan, Ahmadu Bello University, University of Lagos and University of Nigeria Nsukka ("first generation universities"). Provides undergraduate and postgraduate programs across faculties. Operates medical school training doctors. Offers sciences, engineering, social sciences, arts, law, agriculture. Conducts world-class research across disciplines. Operates Obafemi Awolowo University Teaching Hospital. Known for beautiful campus architecture and natural environment. Strong alumni network including prominent Nigerians. Faces challenges from funding constraints, infrastructure decay, ASUU strikes disrupting academics and brain drain of lecturers. OAU maintains academic reputation despite challenges. Critical for tertiary education excellence, research and training Nigeria's professional elite.

**Core Mandate:** To provide excellent university education, conduct high-quality research, train professionals across disciplines and maintain academic excellence as premier Nigerian university.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide undergraduate university education across disciplines
(b) Offer postgraduate programs (Masters, PhD) in all faculties
(c) Operate medical school training doctors and dentists
(d) Provide sciences, engineering and technology education
(e) Offer social sciences, arts and humanities programs
(f) Operate law school and legal education
(g) Provide agriculture, pharmacy and environmental sciences
(h) Conduct world-class research across disciplines
(i) Operate Obafemi Awolowo Teaching Hospital Complex
(j) Maintain status as "first generation" premier university
(k) Uphold academic excellence and research culture
(l) Train Nigeria's professional and intellectual elite
(m) Produce graduates for economy and development
(n) Maintain beautiful campus and natural environment
(o) Build strong alumni network of prominent Nigerians
(p) Address chronic university underfunding
(q) Combat infrastructure decay and facility deterioration
(r) Navigate ASUU strikes disrupting academic calendars
(s) Address lecturer brain drain to foreign universities
(t) Advance tertiary education excellence maintaining premier university status despite funding constraints and ASUU strike disruptions

## 653. NATIONAL MATHEMATICAL CENTRE (NMC), SHEDA

**MDA Code:** 517021025

National Mathematical Centre is federal research institution conducting mathematical research and promoting mathematics education. Located in Sheda, Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Science and Technology. Established to advance mathematical sciences, conduct research and build Nigeria's mathematical capacity. Conducts research in pure and applied mathematics. Researches mathematical modeling and computational mathematics. Conducts operations research and optimization. Researches statistics and probability theory. Develops mathematical models for real-world problems. Conducts research on mathematics education and pedagogy. Promotes mathematics in science, technology and engineering. Provides training for mathematics teachers. Conducts workshops and conferences on mathematical sciences. Publishes mathematical research and journals. Collaborates with mathematics departments in universities. Promotes mathematics olympiads and competitions for students. Addresses poor mathematics performance - WAEC results show high failure rates. Mathematical sciences critical for STEM development, technology and innovation. Faces challenges from limited funding, brain drain of mathematicians and weak mathematics education. Critical for mathematical research, STEM development and improving Nigeria's mathematical capacity supporting science and technology advancement.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct mathematical research, promote mathematics education, build mathematical capacity, support STEM development and advance Nigeria's mathematical sciences.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct research in pure and applied mathematics
(b) Research mathematical modeling and computational mathematics
(c) Conduct operations research and optimization studies
(d) Research statistics, probability and stochastic processes
(e) Develop mathematical models for real-world problem solving
(f) Research mathematics education and teaching methodologies
(g) Promote mathematics in science, technology and engineering applications
(h) Provide training for mathematics teachers and educators
(i) Conduct workshops and conferences on mathematical sciences
(j) Organize mathematics olympiads and competitions for students
(k) Publish mathematical research in journals and proceedings
(l) Collaborate with university mathematics departments
(m) Promote mathematical thinking and problem-solving skills
(n) Address poor mathematics performance in schools
(o) Support STEM education through mathematics promotion
(p) Conduct public lectures on mathematics and its applications
(q) Engage international mathematical organizations
(r) Promote women in mathematics reducing gender gaps
(s) Address mathematics teacher shortages and training gaps
(t) Advance mathematical sciences and STEM capacity building mathematics research and education supporting Nigeria's science, technology and innovation development

## 638. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR NIGERIAN LANGUAGES (NINLAN)

**MDA Code:** 517021028

National Institute for Nigerian Languages is federal institution promoting Nigerian languages, linguistics research and language preservation. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to preserve, develop and promote Nigeria's indigenous languages. Conducts research on Nigerian languages including Hausa, Yoruba, Igbo and minority languages. Develops orthographies and writing systems for unwritten languages. Promotes Nigerian languages teaching and learning. Develops language teaching materials and dictionaries. Conducts linguistic documentation of endangered languages. Researches language endangerment and language loss. Promotes mother-tongue education. Develops curriculum for Nigerian languages instruction. Trains language teachers and linguists. Promotes multilingualism and linguistic diversity. Researches language policy and planning. Documents oral literature and folklore. Collaborates with linguistics departments and language communities. Addresses language extinction - many Nigerian languages endangered from dominant language pressure. Nigeria has 500+ languages requiring documentation and preservation. Promotes national languages (Hausa, Yoruba, Igbo) alongside minority language preservation. Critical for linguistic diversity, cultural heritage and preventing language extinction.

**Core Mandate:** To preserve and promote Nigerian languages, conduct linguistic research, develop language materials, prevent language extinction and advance multilingualism and mother-tongue education.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Preserve and promote Nigeria's 500+ indigenous languages
(b) Conduct research on major Nigerian languages (Hausa, Yoruba, Igbo)
(c) Research minority and endangered Nigerian languages
(d) Develop orthographies for unwritten languages
(e) Create writing systems for oral languages
(f) Promote Nigerian languages teaching in schools
(g) Develop language teaching materials and textbooks
(h) Compile dictionaries for Nigerian languages
(i) Conduct linguistic documentation of endangered languages
(j) Research language endangerment and language loss
(k) Promote mother-tongue education and bilingual education
(l) Develop curriculum for Nigerian languages instruction
(m) Train language teachers and linguists
(n) Promote multilingualism and linguistic diversity
(o) Research language policy and language planning
(p) Document oral literature, folklore and traditional knowledge
(q) Promote language rights and linguistic minorities
(r) Collaborate with language communities on preservation
(s) Engage international linguistics organizations
(t) Advance linguistic diversity and language preservation preventing extinction of Nigeria's 500+ languages through research, documentation and promotion of mother-tongue education

## 722. NIGERIA MARITIME UNIVERSITY, OKERENKOKO

**MDA Code:** 517021042

Nigeria Maritime University Okerenkoko is specialized federal university focusing on maritime education and marine sciences. Located in Okerenkoko, Delta State in Niger Delta, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Recently established as Nigeria's first maritime university. Focuses on maritime studies, marine engineering and nautical sciences. Offers undergraduate programs in maritime transport and logistics. Provides marine engineering and naval architecture programs. Trains in port and shipping management. Offers maritime law and maritime business programs. Provides oceanography and marine sciences education. Supports Nigeria's maritime sector workforce development. Trains for merchant navy, shipping companies and ports. Promotes Cabotage Act implementation requiring Nigerian maritime professionals. Collaborates with Maritime Academy Oron and NIMASA. Located in Niger Delta oil-producing region. Addresses shortage of Nigerian maritime professionals. Faces challenges from establishing new university, limited maritime industry linkages and infrastructure development. Critical for maritime education, shipping sector workforce and supporting Nigeria's maritime industry development through specialized university training.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized maritime education, train maritime professionals, support shipping industry, develop marine sciences and advance Nigeria's maritime sector capacity.

(b) Focus on maritime studies and nautical sciences
(c) Offer undergraduate programs in maritime transport and logistics
(d) Provide marine engineering and ship engineering programs
(e) Teach naval architecture and ship design
(f) Train in port management and shipping operations
(g) Offer maritime law and admiralty law programs
(h) Provide maritime business and shipping management education
(i) Teach oceanography and marine sciences
(j) Provide fisheries and aquaculture programs
(k) Support Nigeria's maritime sector workforce development
(l) Train for merchant navy careers
(m) Provide education for shipping companies and ports
(n) Promote Cabotage Act compliance requiring Nigerian professionals
(o) Collaborate with Maritime Academy Oron on maritime training
(p) Coordinate with NIMASA on maritime education standards
(q) Address shortage of Nigerian maritime professionals
(r) Develop maritime research capacity
(s) Promote blue economy development
(t) Advance maritime sector capacity through specialized university education addressing Nigerian seafarers shortage supporting shipping industry and Cabotage implementation

## 736. NIGERIAN ARMY UNIVERSITY, BIU

**MDA Code:** 517021044

Nigerian Army University is specialized federal military university providing education for military and civilians. Located in Biu, Borno State (Boko Haram-affected region), operating under Nigerian Army and Federal Ministry of Education. Established as first Nigerian military university combining military and civilian education. Admits military personnel and civilians offering degree programs. Provides military-standard education with discipline. Offers programs in sciences, engineering, social sciences and humanities. Operates in insurgency-affected Borno State demonstrating resilience. Promotes education despite northeastern security challenges. Provides employment for Borno residents. Symbolic presence in Boko Haram heartland - militants oppose Western education ("Boko Haram" means "Western education is forbidden"). University represents rejection of insurgent ideology. Faces challenges from security threats, establishing new university and limited infrastructure. Location strategic but challenging. Critical for education access in insurgency-affected region, military-civilian education integration and symbolic defiance of Boko Haram anti-education ideology.

**Core Mandate:** To provide military and civilian university education, operate in insurgency-affected region, promote education despite insecurity and demonstrate resilience against anti-education extremism.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide university education for military personnel and civilians
(b) Admit military officers and civilian students
(c) Operate in Biu, Borno State despite Boko Haram insurgency
(d) Provide military-standard education with discipline
(e) Offer undergraduate programs in sciences and engineering
(f) Provide social sciences and humanities education
(g) Teach military science and strategic studies
(h) Operate despite northeastern Nigeria security challenges
(i) Symbolically reject Boko Haram anti-education ideology
(j) Promote education in insurgency-affected region

## 695. NATIONAL UNIVERSITY OF SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY (NUST), ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 517021057

National University of Science and Technology Abuja is specialized federal university focusing on science, technology and innovation. Located in Abuja, FCT, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Recently established as Nigeria's premier science and technology university. Focuses on science, technology, engineering and mathematics (STEM). Offers undergraduate programs in engineering, pure sciences and technology. Provides postgraduate programs in advanced scientific fields. Conducts cutting-edge research on science and technology. Develops innovation and entrepreneurship programs. Promotes applied sciences and technology commercialization. Operates modern laboratories and research facilities. Collaborates with industries on technology development. Trains scientists, engineers and technologists for 21st century. Promotes fourth industrial revolution technologies. Researches artificial intelligence, robotics and emerging technologies. Supports Nigeria's technology and innovation ecosystem. Addresses STEM skills gaps and technology deficit. Represents government commitment to science and technology education. Faces challenges from establishing new university infrastructure, attracting quality faculty and competing with established universities. Critical for STEM education advancement, technology workforce development and supporting Nigeria's science and technology ambitions.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized science and technology education, conduct advanced research, train STEM professionals, promote innovation and advance Nigeria's scientific and technological capacity.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide specialized science and technology university education
(b) Focus on STEM (Science, Technology, Engineering, Mathematics)

(i) Promote technology commercialization and startups

(n) Research artificial intelligence and machine learning
(o) Study robotics and automation technologies
(p) Research renewable energy and sustainable technologies
(q) Support Nigeria's technology and innovation ecosystem
(r) Address STEM skills gaps in Nigerian workforce
(s) Attract quality faculty and researchers
(t) Advance STEM education producing scientists, engineers and technologists supporting Nigeria's technological development and fourth industrial revolution readiness

# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 661-680

## 661. NATIONAL OPEN UNIVERSITY OF NIGERIA (NOUN)

**MDA Code:** 517024001

National Open University of Nigeria is federal open and distance learning university providing flexible tertiary education. Headquartered in Abuja with study centers nationwide, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established in 1983, closed in 1984, re-established in 2002 as Nigeria's only single-mode open and distance learning (ODL) university. Provides undergraduate and postgraduate degree programs through distance learning. Admits working adults, professionals and non-traditional students. Offers flexible learning accommodating work schedules. Provides programs in arts, sciences, education, management sciences, law, agriculture and engineering. Operates study centers across Nigeria for student support. Delivers courses through print materials, online platforms and multimedia. Conducts virtual lectures and e-learning. Operates facilitation centers for practical sessions and examinations. Admits over 500,000 students making it Africa's largest single-mode ODL university. Provides affordable tertiary education for those unable to attend conventional universities. Promotes lifelong learning and continuing education. Addresses access barriers - working adults, married women, rural residents. Faces challenges from quality concerns, limited practical training, inadequate ICT infrastructure and student support needs. Critical for expanding tertiary education access, democratizing university education and providing flexible learning pathways.

**Core Mandate:** To provide open and distance learning university education, expand tertiary access through flexible programs, serve working adults and non-traditional students, and democratize higher education.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide open and distance learning university education for Nigerians
(b) Admit working adults, professionals and non-traditional students
(c) Offer flexible degree programs accommodating work schedules
(d) Provide undergraduate programs in arts, sciences and social sciences
(e) Offer postgraduate programs (Masters and PhD) through distance learning
(f) Deliver programs in education, management, law, agriculture and engineering
(g) Operate study centers nationwide for student support services
(h) Deliver courses through print materials, course guides and textbooks
(i) Provide online learning platforms and e-learning systems
(j) Conduct virtual lectures and video-based instruction
(k) Operate facilitation centers for practical sessions and examinations
(l) Admit 500,000+ students as Africa's largest single-mode ODL university
(m) Provide affordable tertiary education for disadvantaged groups
(n) Promote lifelong learning and continuing education
(o) Address access barriers for working adults and married women
(p) Serve rural residents unable to relocate to conventional universities
(q) Provide teacher professional development through distance programs
(r) Conduct research on open and distance learning pedagogy
(s) Collaborate with international open universities
(t) Advance tertiary education access democratizing university education through flexible distance learning serving 500,000+ students unable to attend conventional universities

UNESCO National Commission represents Nigeria at United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization. Based in Paris, France. Operates under Federal Ministry of Education and Foreign Affairs. Coordinates Nigeria's participation in UNESCO programs. Accesses UNESCO support for education, science and culture.

## 597. NATIONAL BOARD FOR TECHNICAL EDUCATION (NBTE)

**MDA Code:** 517029001

National Board for Technical Education is apex federal regulatory body overseeing technical and vocational education. Headquartered in Kaduna, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to regulate polytechnics, monotechnics, technical colleges and vocational training institutions. Accredits academic programs in polytechnics ensuring quality standards. Develops curriculum for National Diploma and Higher National Diploma programs. Sets minimum academic standards for technical education. Conducts accreditation visits inspecting facilities, staffing and programs. Regulates Federal, State and Private polytechnics nationwide. Oversees technical colleges and vocational training centers. Develops competency-based training curricula. Promotes Technical and Vocational Education and Training (TVET). Issues guidelines for establishing polytechnics. Collaborates with industries on curriculum development and practical training. Addresses challenges from inadequate funding, poor facilities and TVET perception issues. HND-degree dichotomy limiting polytechnic graduates' career progression remains contentious. Critical for technical workforce development, skills training and polytechnic education regulation ensuring quality technical and vocational training.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate technical and vocational education, accredit polytechnic programs, set TVET standards, develop technical curricula and ensure quality technical education in Nigeria.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate technical and vocational education and training (TVET) in Nigeria
(b) Accredit academic programs in Federal, State and Private polytechnics
(c) Develop curriculum for National Diploma (ND) and Higher National Diploma (HND)
(d) Set minimum academic standards for technical education
(e) Conduct accreditation visits to polytechnics and monotechnics
(f) Inspect facilities, laboratories and workshop equipment
(g) Assess staffing qualifications and staff-student ratios
(h) Regulate technical colleges and vocational training centers
(i) Develop competency-based training curricula
(j) Issue guidelines for establishing polytechnics
(k) Approve new academic programs in technical institutions
(l) Promote industry-institution linkages for practical training
(m) Develop entrepreneurship education in technical programs
(n) Address HND-degree dichotomy affecting polytechnic graduates
(o) Promote TVET as viable alternative to university education
(p) Train technical teachers and instructors
(q) Collaborate with industries on curriculum relevance
(r) Coordinate Students Industrial Work Experience Scheme (SIWES)
(s) Engage international TVET bodies and agencies
(t) Advance technical education quality and TVET development ensuring polytechnics produce skilled technicians and technologists for Nigeria's industrial and technological workforce

## 596. NATIONAL BOARD FOR ARABIC AND ISLAMIC STUDIES (NBAIS)

**MDA Code:** 517030001

National Board for Arabic and Islamic Studies is federal regulatory agency overseeing Arabic and Islamic education. Headquartered in Nigeria, operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to regulate, standardize and develop Arabic and Islamic education in Nigeria's educational system. Regulates Islamiyya schools, Arabic schools and Islamic education institutions. Develops curriculum for Arabic and Islamic studies. Sets standards for Arabic and Islamic teachers. Accredits Islamic education programs in schools. Conducts examinations for Arabic and Islamic studies students. Issues certificates for Quranic education and Arabic proficiency. Promotes integration of Islamiyya education with Western education. Develops teaching materials for Arabic and Islamic subjects. Trains teachers of Arabic and Islamic studies. Collaborates with Islamic universities and Arabic language centers. Addresses challenges from unregulated Almajiri system, standardization gaps and quality concerns. Serves Nigeria's large Muslim population (~50% of population) requiring quality Islamic education. Critical for Islamic education development, Arabic language promotion and integrating religious education with conventional schooling.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate Arabic and Islamic education, develop Islamic studies curriculum, set standards for Islamic schools, promote Arabic language and integrate religious education with conventional system.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate Arabic and Islamic education in Nigeria's education system
(b) Develop curriculum for Arabic and Islamic studies
(c) Set standards for Islamiyya schools and Arabic schools
(d) Accredit Islamic education programs and institutions
(e) Regulate Quranic education and memorization programs
(f) Conduct examinations for Arabic and Islamic studies
(g) Issue certificates for Quranic education completion
(h) Promote integration of Islamiyya with Western education
(i) Develop teaching materials for Islamic studies
(j) Train teachers of Arabic and Islamic education
(k) Set qualification standards for Arabic teachers
(l) Promote Arabic language learning and proficiency
(m) Address Almajiri system challenges and reforms
(n) Standardize Islamic education across states
(o) Promote quality Islamic education
(p) Collaborate with Islamic universities and Arabic centers
(q) Coordinate with education ministries on Islamic curriculum
(r) Engage Saudi Arabia and Islamic countries on Arabic education
(s) Develop policies on Islamic education development
(t) Advance Islamic education quality, Arabic language promotion and integration of religious education serving Nigeria's Muslim population

## 684. NATIONAL SENIOR SECONDARY EDUCATION COMMISSION (NSSEC)

**MDA Code:** 517032001

National Senior Secondary Education Commission is federal regulatory body overseeing senior secondary education. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Recently established to regulate, standardize and improve senior secondary schools (SS1-SS3). Regulates senior secondary education across Federal, State and Private schools. Develops curriculum for senior secondary education. Sets academic standards for SS1-SS3 classes. Accredits senior secondary schools ensuring quality. Monitors school facilities, laboratories and teaching resources. Regulates teachers' qualifications for secondary education. Promotes STEM education in secondary schools. Addresses examination malpractice in secondary schools. Coordinates with WAEC, NECO and NABTEB on examinations. Promotes ICT integration in secondary education. Addresses poor learning outcomes - WAEC results show high failure rates. Promotes science and mathematics education quality. Collaborates with states on secondary education improvement. Addresses challenges from inadequate science laboratories, teacher shortages (especially sciences/mathematics), poor infrastructure and quality gaps. Secondary education critical for university preparation and workforce development. NSSEC represents increased focus on secondary education regulation similar to UBEC for basic education.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate senior secondary education, set academic standards, accredit schools, improve quality and ensure effective SS1-SS3 education across Nigeria.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate senior secondary education (SS1-SS3) across Nigeria
(b) Regulate Federal, State and Private senior secondary schools
(c) Develop curriculum for senior secondary education
(d) Set academic standards and benchmarks for SS1-SS3
(e) Accredit senior secondary schools ensuring quality standards
(f) Monitor school facilities, classrooms and infrastructure
(g) Assess science laboratories and practical facilities
(h) Regulate teachers' qualifications for secondary education
(i) Promote STEM (Science, Technology, Engineering, Mathematics) education
(j) Address examination malpractice in secondary schools
(k) Coordinate with WAEC on West African Senior School Certificate
(l) Collaborate with NECO on Senior School Certificate Examinations
(m) Work with NABTEB on technical secondary education
(n) Promote ICT integration in secondary schools
(o) Address poor learning outcomes and WAEC failure rates
(p) Improve science and mathematics education quality
(q) Promote practical science teaching and experiments
(r) Collaborate with states on secondary education standards
(s) Address teacher shortages especially in sciences and mathematics
(t) Advance secondary education quality through regulation ensuring students receive effective SS1-SS3 education preparing for WAEC/NECO and tertiary admission

## 608. NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR ALMAJIRI AND OUT-OF-SCHOOL CHILDREN EDUCATION

**MDA Code:** 517033001

National Commission for Almajiri and Out-of-School Children Education is federal agency addressing out-of-school children crisis and Almajiri education. Operating under Federal Ministry of Education. Established to tackle Nigeria's estimated 10-20 million out-of-school children challenge, largest number globally. Coordinates programs for Almajiri children (Islamic students in northern Nigeria living as street beggars). Promotes integration of Almajiri system with formal education. Establishes Almajiri Model Schools combining Islamic and Western education. Addresses out-of-school children from poverty, child labor and insecurity. Promotes girls' education in northern Nigeria. Collaborates with states on school enrollment campaigns. Provides alternative education programs for overage children. Supports adult literacy programs. Addresses barriers including poverty, cultural resistance and insecurity preventing school attendance. Almajiri system has millions of children receiving only Quranic education while begging for food. Boko Haram insurgency displaced children from schools. Critical for education access, child protection and addressing Nigeria's out-of-school children crisis threatening development.

**Core Mandate:** To address out-of-school children crisis, integrate Almajiri education with formal schooling, promote enrollment, and ensure every Nigerian child accesses education.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Address Nigeria's out-of-school children crisis (10-20 million children)
(b) Coordinate programs for Almajiri children in northern Nigeria
(c) Integrate Almajiri Islamic education with Western education
(d) Establish Almajiri Model Schools combining religious and secular education
(e) Promote enrollment of out-of-school children
(f) Address barriers including poverty preventing school attendance
(g) Combat child labor keeping children from schools
(h) Promote girls' education in northern Nigeria
(i) Address cultural resistance to Western education
(j) Coordinate with states on school enrollment campaigns
(k) Provide alternative education for overage children
(l) Support accelerated learning programs
(m) Promote adult literacy for out-of-school youth
(n) Address insecurity and Boko Haram impact on school enrollment
(o) Support children displaced by insurgency
(p) Provide school feeding encouraging attendance
(q) Abolish school fees for poor families
(r) Promote community engagement on education importance
(s) Collaborate with religious leaders on Almajiri reforms
(t) Advance universal basic education tackling world's largest out-of-school children population protecting child rights and ensuring education access despite poverty, insurgency and cultural barriers

## 629. NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE SCHEME (NHIS)

**MDA Code:** 521002001

National Health Insurance Scheme is federal health insurance program providing healthcare financing and universal health coverage. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established in 1999 (operational from 2005) to provide affordable healthcare through insurance pooling risk. Operates contributory health insurance for formal sector workers. Provides coverage for enrollees and dependents. Accredits healthcare providers (HMOs and hospitals). Regulates health insurance market. Promotes equity in healthcare access. Addresses out-of-pocket healthcare payments causing impoverishment. Coverage remains low - less than 5% population enrolled despite decades of operation. Formal Sector Social Health Insurance Program (FSSHIP) covers federal civil servants. Informal Sector Social Health Insurance Program targets informal workers, artisans and traders. Vulnerable Group Social Health Insurance Program covers poor and vulnerable. Faces challenges from limited enrollment, inadequate funding, provider quality concerns and implementation gaps. Universal Health Coverage (UHC) goal requires expanding from current ~5% to covering all Nigerians. Healthcare financing reform urgent - Nigeria's health system severely underfunded. Critical for healthcare access, financial protection and achieving UHC.

**Core Mandate:** To provide health insurance coverage, expand enrollment, accredit providers, promote universal health coverage and protect Nigerians from catastrophic health expenditures.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide contributory health insurance for Nigerian workers
(b) Operate Formal Sector Social Health Insurance Program (FSSHIP)
(c) Cover federal civil servants and organized private sector workers
(d) Provide coverage for enrollees' spouses and children
(e) Operate Informal Sector Social Health Insurance Program
(f) Enroll informal workers, artisans, traders and self-employed
(g) Operate Vulnerable Group Social Health Insurance Program
(h) Provide subsidized coverage for poor and vulnerable Nigerians
(i) Accredit Health Maintenance Organizations (HMOs)
(j) Accredit healthcare providers and hospitals
(k) Regulate health insurance market and HMOs
(l) Develop benefit packages covering primary and secondary care
(m) Process and reimburse healthcare claims
(n) Promote equity in healthcare access
(o) Protect enrollees from catastrophic health expenditures
(p) Expand enrollment beyond current ~5% population coverage
(q) Promote universal health coverage (UHC) achievement
(r) Monitor healthcare quality in accredited facilities
(s) Address provider fraud and claim manipulation
(t) Advance universal health coverage and healthcare financing expanding enrollment from 5% toward covering all Nigerians protecting from impoverishing health costs

---

## 675. NATIONAL PRIMARY HEALTH CARE DEVELOPMENT AGENCY (NPHCDA)

**MDA Code:** 521003001

National Primary Health Care Development Agency is federal agency coordinating primary healthcare delivery, immunization and basic health services. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to strengthen primary healthcare system providing basic healthcare to communities. Coordinates National Primary Health Care System across 36 states. Manages routine immunization programs vaccinating children against preventable diseases. Conducts polio eradication campaigns - Nigeria declared polio-free 2020 after decades of efforts. Coordinates COVID-19 vaccination rollout nationwide. Provides maternal and child health services. Operates Midwives Service Scheme deploying midwives to rural areas. Promotes skilled birth attendance and antenatal care. Supports malaria prevention and treatment programs. Provides family planning and reproductive health services. Coordinates nutrition programs addressing malnutrition. Operates community health insurance schemes. Strengthens Primary Health Care Centers (PHCs) infrastructure. Collaborates with WHO, UNICEF, Gavi on immunization. Faces challenges from inadequate PHC facilities (many non-functional), vaccine cold chain gaps, health worker shortages in rural areas and limited funding. PHC critical for universal health coverage but severely underfunded. Critical for basic healthcare, immunization, maternal health and community health services.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate primary healthcare delivery, manage immunization programs, strengthen PHC system, provide maternal/child health services and ensure basic healthcare access nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate National Primary Health Care System across Nigeria
(b) Manage routine immunization programs for children
(c) Conduct polio eradication campaigns (Nigeria polio-free since 2020)
(d) Coordinate COVID-19 vaccination rollout nationwide
(e) Vaccinate children against measles, DPT, BCG and other diseases
(f) Provide maternal health services and antenatal care
(g) Operate Midwives Service Scheme deploying midwives rurally
(h) Promote skilled birth attendance reducing maternal mortality
(i) Support malaria prevention and treatment programs
(j) Provide family planning and reproductive health services
(k) Coordinate nutrition programs addressing child malnutrition
(l) Operate Basic Health Care Provision Fund (BHCPF)
(m) Strengthen Primary Health Care Centers (PHCs) infrastructure
(n) Equip PHCs with essential medicines and supplies
(o) Train community health workers and PHC staff
(p) Promote community health insurance schemes
(q) Collaborate with WHO, UNICEF, Gavi on vaccine delivery
(r) Address vaccine cold chain and logistics challenges
(s) Monitor immunization coverage and disease surveillance
(t) Advance universal health coverage through primary healthcare strengthening PHC system, immunization programs and maternal/child health despite infrastructure gaps and rural health worker shortages

## 591. NATIONAL ARBOVIRUS AND VECTOR RESEARCH CENTRE (NAVRC)

**MDA Code:** 521005001

National Arbovirus and Vector Research Centre is specialized federal research institution studying arboviruses (arthropod-borne viruses) and disease vectors. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Conducts research on mosquito-borne diseases including yellow fever, dengue fever and chikungunya. Studies Lassa fever and other viral hemorrhagic fevers. Researches disease-carrying mosquitoes, ticks and other vectors. Conducts arbovirus surveillance and outbreak investigations. Operates virology laboratory diagnosing arboviral diseases. Studies vector ecology, behavior and control strategies. Researches insecticide resistance in mosquito populations. Develops vector control interventions. Provides technical support during disease outbreaks. Trains public health personnel on arbovirus detection and vector control. Collaborates with NCDC, WHO and international arbovirus research networks. Addresses challenges from emerging arboviral diseases, climate change affecting vector distribution and limited laboratory capacity. Critical for epidemic preparedness, vector-borne disease control and protecting Nigeria from arboviral outbreaks including yellow fever endemic in Nigeria.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct arbovirus research, study disease vectors, provide arboviral disease surveillance, develop vector control strategies and support outbreak prevention and response.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct research on arboviruses including yellow fever, dengue and chikungunya
(b) Study mosquito-borne viral diseases affecting Nigeria
(c) Research Lassa fever and viral hemorrhagic fevers
(d) Study disease-carrying mosquitoes including Aedes and Anopheles species
(e) Research ticks, sandflies and other disease vectors
(f) Conduct arbovirus surveillance and epidemiological monitoring
(g) Investigate arboviral disease outbreaks and epidemics
(h) Operate virology laboratory for arbovirus diagnosis
(i) Study vector ecology, distribution and breeding patterns
(j) Research mosquito behavior and biting patterns
(k) Develop vector control strategies and interventions
(l) Study insecticide resistance in mosquito populations
(m) Research biological control of disease vectors
(n) Develop integrated vector management approaches
(o) Provide technical support during yellow fever and dengue outbreaks
(p) Train public health personnel on arbovirus detection
(q) Conduct environmental health research on vector habitats
(r) Collaborate with NCDC on outbreak response
(s) Engage WHO and international arbovirus research networks
(t) Advance arboviral disease prevention and epidemic preparedness through vector research supporting Nigeria's defense against yellow fever, dengue and emerging arboviral threats

Radiographers Registration Board is federal regulatory body for radiography and medical imaging professions. Operates under Federal Ministry of Health. Registers radiographers and radiologic technologists. Accredits radiography programs in universities and polytechnics. Licenses pharmacies and medical imaging facilities.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate radiography profession, register radiographers, ensure medical imaging standards and promote radiation safety.

## 792. OPTOMETRISTS AND DISPENSING OPTICIANS REGISTRATION BOARD OF NIGERIA

**MDA Code:** 521009001

Optometrists and Dispensing Opticians Registration Board is federal regulatory body for optometry profession. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to regulate optometry practice, register optometrists and ensure eye care standards. Regulates optometry and optical dispensing professions. Registers optometrists and dispensing opticians. Licenses practicing eye care professionals. Accredits optometry programs in universities. Sets optometry education and training standards. Conducts professional examinations and certification. Enforces optometry ethics and professional conduct. Addresses eye care malpractice and quackery. Promotes vision screening and eye health. Collaborates with National Eye Centre and ophthalmologists. Addresses challenges from limited optometrists (shortage of eye care professionals), unregistered optical shops, fake eyeglasses and inadequate regulation. Many Nigerians lack access to vision care. Uncorrected refractive errors common. Critical for eye care regulation, professional standards and ensuring quality vision care despite workforce shortages.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate optometry profession, register optometrists and opticians, ensure eye care standards, prevent quackery and promote vision health nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate optometry and optical dispensing professions
(b) Register qualified optometrists nationwide
(c) Register dispensing opticians and optical technicians
(d) License practicing eye care professionals
(e) Issue annual practicing licenses and renewals
(f) Accredit optometry programs in universities
(g) Set optometry education and training standards
(h) Conduct professional examinations and certification
(i) Enforce optometry ethics and professional conduct codes
(j) Address eye care malpractice and professional misconduct
(k) Combat quackery and unregistered optical practice
(l) Regulate optical shops and eyeglass dispensing
(m) Address fake eyeglasses and substandard optical products
(n) Promote vision screening and eye health awareness
(o) Collaborate with National Eye Centre on eye care
(p) Work with ophthalmologists on integrated eye services
(q) Address severe shortage of optometrists in Nigeria
(r) Combat unregistered optical shops and practitioners
(s) Promote access to affordable vision care
(t) Advance eye care regulation ensuring quality vision services despite optometrist shortages and quackery challenges

## 779. NURSING AND MIDWIFERY COUNCIL OF NIGERIA (NMCN)

**MDA Code:** 521011001

Nursing and Midwifery Council of Nigeria is federal regulatory body for nursing and midwifery professions. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to regulate nursing practice, register nurses/midwives and ensure professional standards. Regulates nursing and midwifery professions nationwide. Registers qualified nurses and midwives. Licenses practicing nurses and midwives. Accredits Schools of Nursing and Schools of Midwifery. Sets nursing education standards and curricula. Conducts nursing examinations and certification. Issues annual practicing licenses. Enforces nursing ethics and professional conduct. Addresses nursing malpractice and incompetence. Promotes nursing education quality. Collaborates with teaching hospitals on nurse training. Addresses challenges from nurse shortages (Nigeria has ~200,000+ registered nurses for 200M+ population - massive deficit), inadequate nursing schools, nurse migration abroad (brain drain to UK, US, Canada) and quality concerns. Nurse-patient ratios poor. Critical for nursing regulation, professional standards and ensuring quality nursing care despite severe workforce shortages and brain drain.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate nursing and midwifery, register practitioners, accredit nursing schools, enforce professional standards and ensure quality nursing practice nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate nursing and midwifery professions in Nigeria
(b) Register qualified nurses and midwives nationally
(c) License practicing nurses and midwives
(d) Issue annual practicing licenses and renewals
(e) Accredit Schools of Nursing nationwide
(f) Accredit Schools of Midwifery and community health
(g) Set nursing education standards and curricula
(h) Develop minimum academic standards for nursing programs
(i) Conduct final nursing examinations and certification
(j) Enforce nursing ethics and professional conduct
(k) Address nursing malpractice and professional misconduct
(l) Investigate complaints against nurses
(m) Impose sanctions including license suspension
(n) Promote nursing education quality and standards
(o) Collaborate with teaching hospitals on clinical training
(p) Address severe nurse shortages (~200,000 for 200M+ population)
(q) Combat nurse migration and brain drain abroad
(r) Address nurses leaving for UK, US, Canada opportunities
(s) Improve nurse-patient ratios currently inadequate
(t) Advance nursing regulation and quality despite critical shortages and brain drain threatening healthcare workforce

---

## 797. PHARMACISTS COUNCIL OF NIGERIA (PCN)

**MDA Code:** 521012001

Pharmacists Council of Nigeria is federal regulatory body for pharmacy profession. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to regulate pharmacy practice, register pharmacists and ensure pharmaceutical standards. Regulates pharmacy profession and pharmaceutical practice. Registers pharmacists and pharmacy technicians. Issues annual practicing licenses for pharmacists. Accredits pharmacy programs in universities. Sets pharmacy education and training standards. Licenses pharmacies, patent medicine stores and drug outlets. Enforces pharmaceutical ethics and professional conduct. Addresses pharmacy malpractice and drug dispensing errors. Combats fake drug sales and counterfeit pharmaceuticals. Regulates drug importation and distribution. Collaborates with NAFDAC on drug safety. Addresses challenges from illegal drug sellers, unregistered pharmacies, fake drugs and pharmacist shortages. Many patent medicine vendors operate illegally. Drug counterfeiting widespread. Pharmacist-population ratio inadequate. Critical for pharmaceutical regulation, drug safety and ensuring quality pharmacy practice despite fake drugs and illegal sellers.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate pharmacy profession, register pharmacists, license pharmacies, ensure pharmaceutical standards, combat fake drugs and promote safe pharmaceutical practice.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate pharmacy profession and pharmaceutical practice
(b) Register qualified pharmacists nationwide
(c) Register pharmacy technicians and pharmaceutical technologists
(d) Issue annual practicing licenses for pharmacists
(e) Accredit pharmacy programs in universities
(f) Set pharmacy education and training standards
(g) License retail pharmacies and pharmaceutical outlets
(h) Regulate patent medicine vendors and drug stores
(i) Enforce pharmaceutical ethics and professional conduct
(j) Address pharmacy malpractice and drug dispensing errors
(k) Combat fake drug sales and counterfeit pharmaceuticals
(l) Regulate drug importation and pharmaceutical distribution
(m) Inspect pharmacies and drug outlets for compliance
(n) Collaborate with NAFDAC on drug safety and quality
(o) Address illegal drug sellers and unregistered pharmacies
(p) Combat widespread patent medicine vendor violations
(q) Address drug counterfeiting threatening public health
(r) Tackle inadequate pharmacist-population ratios
(s) Promote rational drug use and pharmaceutical care
(t) Advance pharmaceutical regulation ensuring drug safety combating fake drugs and illegal sellers despite widespread counterfeiting
