# NIGERIAN FEDERAL AGENCY MANDATES: AGENCIES 581-601

## 581. NATIONAL AGENCY FOR FOOD AND DRUG ADMINISTRATION AND CONTROL (NAFDAC)

**MDA Code:** 521014001

National Agency for Food and Drug Administration and Control (NAFDAC) is premier federal regulatory agency ensuring safety and quality of food, drugs, cosmetics and chemicals. Headquartered in Abuja with zonal and state offices nationwide, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established in 1993, NAFDAC gained prominence under late Prof. Dora Akunyili (DG 2001-2008) who revolutionized drug safety eliminating fake drugs. Regulates pharmaceuticals, food products, cosmetics, medical devices, chemicals and packaged water. Registers and approves drugs before market authorization. Conducts laboratory testing on food and drug samples. Inspects manufacturing facilities, warehouses and retail outlets. Combats counterfeit drugs and substandard products endangering public health. Monitors advertisements of regulated products. Enforces Good Manufacturing Practices (GMP) in pharmaceutical companies. Issues NAFDAC numbers certifying product registration. Destroys seized fake and expired products publicly. Operates port inspection preventing dangerous imports. Collaborates with customs, police and international drug regulatory authorities. Faces challenges from sophisticated counterfeiters, porous borders and limited laboratory capacity. Critical for public health, consumer protection and pharmaceutical industry regulation.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate and control manufacture, importation, distribution and sale of food, drugs, cosmetics and chemicals; ensure safety and quality; and protect Nigerians from fake and substandard products.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate food, drugs, cosmetics, medical devices and chemicals in Nigeria
(b) Register and approve pharmaceutical products before market authorization
(c) Issue NAFDAC registration numbers certifying product compliance
(d) Conduct laboratory testing on food and drug samples for quality
(e) Inspect pharmaceutical manufacturing facilities enforcing GMP standards
(f) Monitor food processing plants and packaged water factories
(g) Combat counterfeit and fake drugs endangering public health
(h) Destroy seized fake, expired and substandard products publicly
(i) Inspect warehouses, pharmacies and retail outlets for compliance
(j) Conduct port inspection preventing dangerous imports at borders
(k) Monitor and regulate product advertisements and marketing claims
(l) Enforce labeling requirements on food and drug products
(m) Conduct post-market surveillance detecting substandard products
(n) Investigate adverse drug reactions and product complaints
(o) Prosecute manufacturers and distributors of fake products
(p) Provide consumer education on product safety
(q) Accredit pharmaceutical and food testing laboratories
(r) Collaborate with customs on import inspection
(s) Coordinate with WHO and international drug regulators
(t) Advance public health and consumer protection through rigorous regulation eliminating fake drugs, ensuring food safety and maintaining pharmaceutical quality standards

---

## 673. NATIONAL POSTGRADUATE MEDICAL COLLEGE OF NIGERIA (NPMCN)

**MDA Code:** 521022001

National Postgraduate Medical College of Nigeria is premier medical institution conducting postgraduate medical education and specialist training. Located in Ijanikin, Lagos State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to train medical specialists through Fellowship programs. Conducts Fellowship examinations for medical specialization. Trains specialists through Fellowship of National Postgraduate Medical College (FNPMC) programs. Accredits hospitals as training centers for residency programs. Provides specialist training in internal medicine, surgery, pediatrics, obstetrics/gynecology and other specialties. Conducts Part 1 and Part 2 Fellowship examinations. Issues Fellowship certificates to qualified specialist doctors. Promotes continuous medical education for specialists. Collaborates with West African College of Surgeons (WACS) and West African College of Physicians. Addresses specialist doctor shortages - Nigeria has critical specialist gaps. Many doctors pursue postgraduate training abroad (UK, US, Canada) contributing to brain drain. NPMCN provides local alternative to foreign postgraduate medical training. Faces challenges from limited training facilities, examination capacity and specialist trainer shortages. Critical for specialist medical workforce development, reducing foreign medical training dependence and addressing Nigeria's specialist doctor shortage.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct postgraduate medical education, train medical specialists, conduct Fellowship examinations, accredit training hospitals and develop Nigeria's specialist medical workforce.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Conduct postgraduate medical education and specialist training
(b) Provide Fellowship programs training medical specialists
(c) Conduct Fellowship examinations (Part 1 and Part 2)
(d) Award Fellowship of National Postgraduate Medical College (FNPMC)
(e) Train specialists in internal medicine and medical subspecialties
(f) Provide surgical specialty training and examinations
(g) Conduct pediatrics and child health specialist training
(h) Train obstetrics and gynecology specialists
(i) Provide anesthesia and intensive care specialist training
(j) Train pathologists, radiologists and laboratory medicine specialists
(k) Accredit hospitals as training centers for residency programs
(l) Inspect and monitor training facilities in accredited hospitals
(m) Promote continuous medical education for specialists
(n) Conduct specialist refresher courses and workshops
(o) Collaborate with West African College of Surgeons (WACS)
(p) Coordinate with West African College of Physicians
(q) Address specialist doctor shortages in Nigeria
(r) Provide local alternative to foreign postgraduate training
(s) Reduce medical brain drain through local specialist training
(t) Advance specialist medical education training Nigerian doctors locally reducing foreign training dependence and brain drain addressing critical specialist shortages

Operates as major referral hospital for southwestern Nigeria.

## 664. NATIONAL ORTHOPAEDIC HOSPITAL, IGBOBI, LAGOS

**MDA Code:** 521027014

National Orthopaedic Hospital Igbobi Lagos is premier federal orthopedic hospital and oldest specialized orthopedic institution. Located in Igbobi, Lagos State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established in 1945 making it Nigeria's oldest and most established orthopedic hospital. Provides comprehensive orthopedic surgery and trauma care. Conducts complex joint replacement surgeries including hip and knee replacements. Performs spinal surgery for complex spinal conditions. Provides pediatric orthopedics for children's musculoskeletal disorders. Treats polio complications and paralytic conditions. Conducts hand surgery and microsurgery. Provides sports medicine and sports injury treatment. Operates comprehensive physiotherapy and rehabilitation center. Provides prosthetics and orthotics workshop. Trains orthopedic surgeons - most Nigerian orthopedic surgeons trained at or rotated through Igbobi. Serves as national referral center for complex orthopedic cases. Addresses high burden of road traffic accidents in Lagos metropolis. Treats Pott's disease (spinal tuberculosis) and bone tuberculosis. Faces challenges from aging infrastructure despite historical prestige, overwhelming patient numbers and equipment needs. Critical for orthopedic training, complex surgery and serving as Nigeria's premier orthopedic institution.

**Core Mandate:** To provide premier orthopedic surgery and trauma care, conduct complex procedures, train orthopedic surgeons, serve as national referral center and maintain Nigeria's oldest orthopedic hospital legacy.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide comprehensive orthopedic surgery as premier national hospital (established 1945)
(b) Conduct complex joint replacement surgeries (hip, knee, shoulder)
(c) Perform advanced spinal surgery for complex spinal conditions
(d) Provide pediatric orthopedics for children's bone and joint disorders
(e) Treat polio complications and paralytic orthopedic conditions
(f) Conduct hand surgery and microsurgical procedures
(g) Provide sports medicine and athletic injury treatment
(h) Operate comprehensive physiotherapy and rehabilitation services
(9) Provide prosthetics and orthotics manufacturing and fitting
(j) Manage bone and joint infections including osteomyelitis
(k) Treat Pott's disease (spinal tuberculosis) and bone TB
(l) Train orthopedic surgeons - national training center
(m) Provide orthopedic residency programs
(n) Serve as national referral center for complex orthopedic cases
(o) Address high burden of road traffic injuries in Lagos
(p) Conduct orthopedic research and innovation
(q) Collaborate with international orthopedic institutions
(r) Provide emergency trauma services
(s) Maintain historical role as Nigeria's first orthopedic hospital
(t) Advance orthopedic care as premier national institution training most Nigerian orthopedic surgeons and managing complex cases despite aging infrastructure and overwhelming Lagos patient load

## 665. NATIONAL ORTHOPAEDIC HOSPITAL, ENUGU

**MDA Code:** 521027015

National Orthopaedic Hospital Enugu is specialized federal orthopedic hospital serving southeastern Nigeria. Located in Enugu, Enugu State (former Eastern Region capital), operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of federal orthopedic hospitals network addressing musculoskeletal conditions. Provides orthopedic surgery including fracture management and joint procedures. Conducts trauma surgery from road accidents and injuries. Performs joint replacement surgeries for arthritis patients. Provides spinal surgery for back pain and spinal conditions. Conducts pediatric orthopedics treating children's bone deformities. Offers physiotherapy and rehabilitation services. Provides prosthetics and orthotic devices. Treats bone infections and chronic osteomyelitis. Manages traditional bone setter complications requiring corrective surgery. Trains orthopedic surgeons and physiotherapists. Serves as referral hospital for southeastern states. Addresses musculoskeletal conditions, trauma injuries and road traffic accidents. Southeastern Nigeria's commercial activities generate significant trauma cases. Faces challenges from patient overload, equipment limitations and orthopedic specialist shortages. Critical for southeastern Nigeria orthopedic care, trauma management and specialized musculoskeletal treatment.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized orthopedic and trauma care, conduct joint surgeries, offer rehabilitation services, train orthopedic professionals and serve southeastern Nigeria.

(a) Provide specialized orthopedic surgery for southeastern Nigeria

(c) Perform joint replacement surgeries (hip, knee replacements)
(d) Provide spinal surgery for back pain and spinal disorders
(e) Conduct pediatric orthopedics for children's bone conditions
(f) Treat congenital deformities and developmental disorders
(g) Provide physiotherapy and physical rehabilitation
(h) Offer prosthetics and orthotic devices for disabilities
(i) Treat bone infections, osteomyelitis and chronic bone disease
(j) Manage complications from traditional bone setters
(k) Provide arthroscopy and joint procedures
(l) Conduct sports injury treatment
(m) Train orthopedic surgeons through residency programs
(n) Train physiotherapists and rehabilitation professionals
(o) Serve as tertiary referral for southeastern states
(p) Address road traffic accident injuries
(q) Treat occupational injuries from commercial activities
(r) Conduct orthopedic outreach to rural communities
(s) Operate emergency trauma services
(t) Advance orthopedic care serving southeastern Nigeria providing specialized musculoskeletal treatment, trauma management and training despite patient overload and resource constraints

## 690. NATIONAL TUBERCULOSIS AND LEPROSY TRAINING CENTRE, ZARIA

**MDA Code:** 521027016

National Tuberculosis and Leprosy Training Centre is specialized federal hospital treating TB, leprosy and training healthcare workers. Located in Zaria, Kaduna State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to provide TB/leprosy treatment, conduct training and support national TB control programs. Provides diagnosis and treatment for tuberculosis patients. Treats Multi-Drug Resistant TB (MDR-TB) requiring specialized care. Provides leprosy diagnosis and treatment. Manages leprosy complications and disabilities. Conducts training for healthcare workers on TB/leprosy management. Provides TB laboratory services and drug susceptibility testing. Operates directly observed therapy (DOT) programs. Supports National TB Control Programme implementation. Addresses stigma associated with TB and leprosy. Provides rehabilitation for leprosy-related disabilities. Collaborates with WHO on TB elimination. Nigeria has high TB burden - estimated 440,000+ new TB cases annually, 4th highest burden globally. MDR-TB emerging as serious challenge. Leprosy endemic in some areas though reduced from past. Faces challenges from drug-resistant TB, treatment adherence, stigma and limited resources. Critical for TB control, leprosy elimination and specialized training supporting Nigeria's TB response.

**Core Mandate:** To provide TB and leprosy treatment, train healthcare workers, support national TB control, manage drug-resistant TB and advance TB/leprosy elimination.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide diagnosis and treatment for tuberculosis patients
(b) Treat Multi-Drug Resistant TB (MDR-TB) requiring specialized care
(c) Manage Extensively Drug-Resistant TB (XDR-TB) cases
(d) Provide leprosy diagnosis and treatment services
(e) Manage leprosy complications and disabilities
(f) Conduct training for healthcare workers on TB/leprosy management
(g) Train on TB case detection and notification
(h) Provide TB laboratory services and microscopy
(i) Conduct drug susceptibility testing for TB
(j) Operate directly observed therapy (DOT) programs
(k) Support National TB Control Programme nationwide
(l) Address stigma associated with TB and leprosy
(m) Provide rehabilitation for leprosy-related disabilities
(n) Prevent TB transmission in communities
(o) Conduct TB screening and contact tracing
(p) Collaborate with WHO on TB elimination strategies
(q) Address Nigeria's high TB burden (440,000+ cases, 4th globally)
(r) Manage TB-HIV co-infection
(s) Conduct research on TB and leprosy
(t) Advance TB control and leprosy elimination through specialized treatment and training addressing Nigeria's 440,000+ annual TB cases and MDR-TB challenge

## 625. NATIONAL EYE CENTRE, KADUNA

**MDA Code:** 521027039

National Eye Centre is premier federal specialized ophthalmology hospital providing eye care services. Located in Kaduna, Kaduna State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established as tertiary referral eye hospital for northern Nigeria and beyond. Provides comprehensive eye care including cataract surgery, glaucoma treatment and retinal services. Conducts cataract surgeries restoring vision for blind patients. Treats glaucoma preventing blindness from high eye pressure. Provides diabetic retinopathy services for diabetes complications. Performs corneal transplants and refractive surgery. Treats eye infections, trachoma and river blindness (onchocerciasis). Provides pediatric ophthalmology for children's eye conditions. Operates outreach programs providing free eye screenings and surgeries. Trains ophthalmologists, optometrists and eye care professionals. Conducts research on eye diseases prevalent in Nigeria. Collaborates with international eye care organizations and NGOs. Addresses preventable blindness affecting millions - cataracts, glaucoma and refractive errors cause significant blindness. Faces challenges from overwhelming patient load, limited resources and access barriers for poor patients. Critical for vision restoration, preventing blindness and providing specialized eye care across northern Nigeria.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized eye care services, conduct eye surgeries, prevent blindness, train ophthalmologists and serve as referral eye hospital for northern Nigeria.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide comprehensive eye care and ophthalmology services
(b) Conduct cataract surgeries restoring vision to blind patients
(c) Treat glaucoma preventing blindness from high eye pressure
(d) Provide diabetic retinopathy services for diabetes eye complications
(e) Perform corneal transplants for corneal blindness
(f) Conduct refractive surgery including LASIK
(g) Treat eye infections including conjunctivitis and keratitis
(h) Address trachoma causing blindness in northern communities
(i) Treat river blindness (onchocerciasis) and neglected tropical diseases
(j) Provide pediatric ophthalmology for children's eye conditions
(k) Operate outreach programs providing free eye screenings
(l) Conduct free cataract surgeries for poor patients
(m) Train ophthalmologists and eye surgeons
(n) Train optometrists and ophthalmic nurses
(o) Conduct research on eye diseases in Nigeria
(p) Operate as tertiary referral hospital for northern Nigeria
(q) Collaborate with Sightsavers, Orbis and international eye NGOs
(r) Promote eye health awareness and prevention
(s) Address preventable blindness from cataracts and glaucoma
(t) Advance vision health and blindness prevention providing specialized eye care, surgeries and training combating preventable blindness affecting millions of Nigerians

## 620. NATIONAL EAR CARE CENTRE, KADUNA

**MDA Code:** 521027040

National Ear Care Centre is specialized federal healthcare facility providing ear, nose and throat (ENT) services. Located in Kaduna, Kaduna State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to provide specialized ENT care, audiology services and hearing healthcare. Provides diagnosis and treatment of ear diseases and hearing disorders. Conducts hearing assessments and audiometry testing. Provides hearing aids and assistive listening devices. Treats ear infections, otitis media and hearing loss. Performs ENT surgeries including ear surgeries. Provides services for deaf and hard-of-hearing persons. Conducts newborn hearing screening. Trains ENT specialists, audiologists and hearing professionals. Promotes ear health and hearing conservation. Addresses preventable hearing loss from infections and noise exposure. Provides cochlear implant assessments and management. Conducts community outreach on ear care. Collaborates with schools for deaf and hearing-impaired organizations. Addresses challenges from limited ENT specialists, expensive hearing aids and inadequate services nationwide. Hearing loss affects millions globally; ear infections common in Nigerian children. Critical for hearing healthcare, preventing deafness and supporting persons with hearing disabilities.

**Core Mandate:** To provide specialized ear care and ENT services, diagnose and treat hearing disorders, provide hearing aids, train professionals and promote ear health nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**

(b) Diagnose and treat ear diseases and hearing disorders
(c) Conduct comprehensive hearing assessments and audiometry
(d) Provide hearing aids and assistive listening devices
(e) Treat ear infections including otitis media
(f) Address hearing loss from infections and noise exposure
(g) Perform ENT surgeries including ear surgeries
(h) Provide services for deaf and hard-of-hearing persons
(i) Conduct newborn hearing screening programs
(j) Provide pediatric audiology services
(k) Train ENT specialists and surgeons
(l) Train audiologists and hearing healthcare professionals
(m) Promote ear health and hearing conservation
(n) Conduct cochlear implant assessments
(o) Provide speech therapy for hearing-impaired
(p) Conduct community ear care outreach programs
(q) Promote hearing protection from noise exposure
(r) Collaborate with schools for deaf children
(s) Engage hearing-impaired organizations
(t) Advance hearing healthcare and ear disease prevention providing specialized ENT services, preventing deafness and supporting millions affected by hearing disorders

## 708. NIGERIA CENTRE FOR DISEASE CONTROL (NCDC), ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 521027047

Nigeria Centre for Disease Control is federal public health agency coordinating disease surveillance, outbreak response and epidemic control. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established 2011, gained prominence during COVID-19 pandemic. Coordinates disease surveillance and outbreak detection nationwide. Responds to disease outbreaks and epidemics. Managed Nigeria's COVID-19 response - testing, contact tracing, isolation centers. Addresses Lassa fever annual outbreaks (endemic in southern Nigeria). Responds to cholera outbreaks during rainy seasons. Manages yellow fever outbreak responses. Coordinates monkeypox and other emerging infectious diseases. Operates National Reference Laboratory. Provides laboratory diagnosis for infectious diseases. Conducts epidemiological investigations. Trains public health professionals on outbreak response. Issues public health advisories and guidelines. Collaborates with WHO, Africa CDC and international health agencies. Operates Emergency Operations Centre. Faces challenges from limited laboratory capacity, funding constraints and health system weaknesses. NCDC leadership during COVID-19 demonstrated importance. Critical for epidemic preparedness, outbreak response and protecting Nigerians from infectious disease threats.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate disease surveillance and outbreak response, manage epidemics, conduct laboratory diagnosis, provide public health leadership and protect Nigeria from infectious diseases.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate disease surveillance and outbreak detection nationwide
(b) Respond to disease outbreaks and epidemics
(c) Manage COVID-19 pandemic response (testing, contact tracing, isolation)
(d) Address annual Lassa fever outbreaks in southern Nigeria
(e) Respond to cholera outbreaks during rainy seasons
(f) Manage yellow fever outbreak responses
(g) Coordinate monkeypox and emerging infectious disease responses
(h) Operate National Reference Laboratory for disease diagnosis
(i) Provide laboratory testing for infectious diseases
(j) Conduct epidemiological investigations and contact tracing
(k) Train public health professionals on outbreak response
(l) Develop disease surveillance systems
(m) Issue public health advisories and prevention guidelines
(n) Operate Emergency Operations Centre coordinating responses
(o) Collaborate with WHO on International Health Regulations
(p) Coordinate with Africa CDC on continental health security
(q) Manage infection prevention and control programs
(r) Conduct research on infectious diseases
(s) Address antimicrobial resistance surveillance
(t) Advance epidemic preparedness and response protecting Nigerians from infectious disease threats through surveillance, laboratory diagnosis and outbreak coordination

## 663. NATIONAL ORTHOPAEDIC HOSPITAL, DALA, KANO

**MDA Code:** 521027063

National Orthopaedic Hospital Dala Kano is specialized federal orthopedic and trauma hospital. Located in Dala, Kano State (Nigeria's second most populous state), operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of network of federal orthopedic hospitals addressing bone, joint and musculoskeletal conditions. Provides specialized orthopedic surgery including fracture management, joint replacements and spinal surgery. Treats trauma injuries from road traffic accidents. Conducts pediatric orthopedics for children's bone conditions. Provides arthroscopy and minimally invasive orthopedic procedures. Treats sports injuries and musculoskeletal disorders. Conducts physiotherapy and rehabilitation services. Provides prosthetics and orthotics for amputees. Trains orthopedic surgeons through residency programs. Serves as tertiary referral hospital for northern Nigeria. Addresses high burden of road traffic injuries, falls and trauma in densely populated Kano. Traditional bone setters common but often cause complications requiring corrective surgery. Faces challenges from overwhelming patient load, limited equipment and orthopedic surgeon shortages. Critical for orthopedic care, trauma management and serving northern Nigeria's musculoskeletal health needs.

## 801. PORT HEALTH SERVICES (PHS), ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 521029001

Port Health Services Abuja operates at Nnamdi Azikiwe International Airport in Federal Capital Territory. Located at Nigeria's second-busiest airport serving capital city. Screens international arrivals, conducts health inspections and enforces quarantine regulations. Capital city entry point handles high diplomatic and business traveler traffic. COVID-19 testing center for international travelers. Verifies yellow fever vaccination certificates. Monitors malaria and infectious disease surveillance at FCT gateway.

**Core Mandate:** To prevent disease importation through Abuja airport, screen international travelers, enforce health regulations and protect FCT from imported infectious diseases.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Screen international travelers arriving at Nnamdi Azikiwe Airport
(b) Verify yellow fever vaccination certificates for entry compliance
(c) Conduct COVID-19 testing and vaccination verification
(d) Enforce International Health Regulations (IHR 2005) at airport
(e) Quarantine suspected infectious disease cases
(f) Issue health certificates for international travel
(g) Monitor disease surveillance at FCT entry point
(h) Inspect aircraft for health hazards and sanitation
(i) Screen airline crew for infectious diseases
(j) Conduct temperature screening for travelers
(k) Test for infectious diseases as required
(l) Monitor WHO disease alerts affecting travelers
(m) Collaborate with NCDC on outbreak response
(n) Coordinate with Immigration on traveler screening
(o) Maintain health screening equipment and facilities
(p) Record and report disease surveillance data
(q) Enforce quarantine for travelers from outbreak areas
(r) Provide public health information to travelers
(s) Inspect food and water on aircraft
(t) Protect Abuja and Nigeria from imported diseases through airport health screening

## 800. PRIMARY HEALTH CENTRE (PHC), ABEOKUTA

**MDA Code:** 521029012

Primary Health Centre Abeokuta is federal primary healthcare facility providing basic health services. Located in Abeokuta, Ogun State capital, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of Nigeria's primary healthcare system providing community-level health services. Provides primary healthcare services to communities. Offers maternal and child health services. Conducts immunization and vaccination programs. Provides family planning and reproductive health. Offers antenatal care and postnatal services. Provides basic disease treatment and management. Conducts health education and promotion. Operates as first-line healthcare facility. Refers complex cases to secondary/tertiary facilities. Addresses preventable diseases through primary care. Collaborates with NPHCDA on programs. Faces challenges from limited staff, equipment shortages, drug stockouts and infrastructure decay affecting many PHCs. Nigeria's PHCs often dysfunctional despite being healthcare foundation. Critical for basic healthcare access, disease prevention and community health despite widespread PHC system challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To provide primary healthcare services, deliver maternal/child health, conduct immunization, offer basic treatment and serve as community first-line healthcare facility.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide primary healthcare services to Abeokuta communities
(b) Offer maternal and child health services
(c) Conduct immunization and vaccination programs
(d) Provide family planning and reproductive health services
(e) Offer antenatal care for pregnant women
(f) Provide postnatal care and newborn services
(g) Deliver basic disease treatment and management
(h) Provide outpatient care for common illnesses
(i) Conduct health education and health promotion
(j) Operate as first-line community healthcare facility
(k) Refer complex cases to general hospitals
(l) Address preventable diseases through primary care
(m) Provide essential medicines and basic drugs
(n) Collaborate with NPHCDA on national programs
(o) Support community health insurance enrollment
(p) Address limited healthcare staff shortages
(q) Tackle equipment and supplies shortages
(r) Combat frequent drug stockouts
(s) Address infrastructure decay in PHC facilities
(t) Advance community healthcare and disease prevention despite widespread PHC system dysfunction and resource challenges

## 812. PORT HEALTH SERVICES (PHS), PORT HARCOURT (BONNY, BRASS, ONNE, AIRPORT, WHARF)

**MDA Code:** 521029013

Port Health Services Port Harcourt operates across Niger Delta oil region at multiple locations. Port Harcourt International Airport serves as international gateway for oil industry. Port Harcourt Wharf conducts seaport operations. Onne Port operates at major oil and gas port screening oil workers. Bonny terminal monitors offshore oil workers. Brass LNG terminal conducts health monitoring. Screens expatriate oil workers. Operates in yellow fever endemic area. Addresses malaria control. Provides offshore platform worker health certification.

**Core Mandate:** To screen oil industry workers, prevent disease importation through Niger Delta entry points and protect oil region from imported diseases.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Screen international travelers at Port Harcourt Airport
(b) Conduct health screening at Port Harcourt Wharf
(c) Operate at Onne oil and gas port
(d) Monitor Bonny oil terminal health
(e) Conduct screening at Brass LNG terminal
(f) Screen expatriate oil workers
(g) Verify yellow fever vaccination in endemic area
(h) Issue offshore worker health certificates
(i) Enforce International Health Regulations
(j) Quarantine suspected disease cases
(k) Screen seafarers and oil platform workers
(l) Inspect ships for health hazards
(m) Monitor malaria in Niger Delta
(n) Conduct COVID-19 testing
(o) Screen airline crew members
(p) Monitor WHO disease alerts
(q) Collaborate with oil companies on health
(r) Coordinate with NCDC on surveillance
(s) Inspect aircraft sanitation
(t) Protect Niger Delta oil region from diseases through comprehensive entry point screening

## 637. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR PHARMACEUTICAL RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT (NIPRD), ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 521031001

National Institute for Pharmaceutical Research and Development is federal institution conducting pharmaceutical and drug research. Located in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to conduct drug research, develop pharmaceutical products and promote indigenous drug manufacturing. Conducts research on medicinal plants and traditional medicine. Develops pharmaceutical formulations from natural products. Researches drug discovery from Nigerian biodiversity. Conducts clinical trials on new drugs. Researches generic drug formulations. Develops herbal medicines and phytopharmaceuticals. Conducts drug quality control and analysis. Researches pharmaceutical manufacturing processes. Develops vaccines and biological products. Promotes local pharmaceutical manufacturing. Provides technical support to pharmaceutical companies. Collaborates with universities and research institutes. Addresses drug importation dependence - Nigeria imports 70%+ drugs. Promotes traditional medicine integration into healthcare. Researches anti-malarial drugs from local plants. Faces challenges from limited commercialization, pharmaceutical industry constraints and drug development costs. Critical for pharmaceutical self-reliance, drug security and reducing import dependence.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct pharmaceutical research, develop drugs from medicinal plants, promote local drug manufacturing, support pharmaceutical industry and reduce drug import dependence.

(b) Research medicinal plants and traditional medicine
(c) Develop pharmaceutical formulations from natural products

(g) Conduct clinical trials on new drug candidates
(h) Research generic drug formulations

(j) Conduct pharmaceutical quality control and drug analysis

(m) Promote local pharmaceutical manufacturing

(o) Integrate traditional medicine into modern healthcare
(p) Conduct toxicology and safety testing
(q) Research drug-resistant diseases treatment
(r) Collaborate with universities and research institutes
(s) Address Nigeria's 70%+ drug import dependence

## 754. NIGERIAN INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL RESEARCH (NIMR), YABA

**MDA Code:** 521032001

Nigerian Institute of Medical Research is premier federal biomedical research institution conducting health research. Located in Yaba, Lagos, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to conduct medical research, develop health interventions and support disease control programs. Conducts biomedical and clinical research. Researches infectious diseases including HIV, TB and malaria. Studies non-communicable diseases (diabetes, hypertension, cancer). Researches maternal and child health. Conducts vaccine research and development. Studies disease epidemiology and surveillance. Researches traditional medicine and herbal remedies. Provides laboratory diagnostic services. Conducts clinical trials on new treatments. Researches health systems and policy. Collaborates with WHO, NIH and international research institutions. Addresses challenges from limited funding, brain drain of researchers and research infrastructure gaps. Nigeria's disease burden requires robust research. Critical for health research, disease control and medical innovations supporting Nigeria's health challenges despite funding and infrastructure constraints.

**Core Mandate:** To conduct biomedical research, study diseases, develop health interventions, support disease control and advance medical science capacity.

(m) Study nutrition and malnutrition impacts
(n) Conduct environmental health research
(o) Research genetic disorders and sickle cell disease
(p) Collaborate with WHO, NIH and international institutions
(q) Address limited research funding constraints
(r) Combat brain drain of medical researchers
(s) Upgrade research infrastructure and equipment
(t) Advance medical research and health innovations addressing Nigeria's disease burden despite funding constraints and infrastructure gaps

## 771. NOMA CHILDREN HOSPITAL, SOKOTO

**MDA Code:** 521036001

Noma Children Hospital is specialized federal hospital treating noma disease in children. Located in Sokoto, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to treat noma (cancrum oris), devastating facial gangrene affecting malnourished children. Noma is neglected tropical disease causing facial tissue destruction. Affects severely malnourished children aged 2-6 years. Causes facial disfigurement and tissue necrosis. Provides surgical reconstruction for noma survivors. Conducts plastic and reconstructive surgery. Addresses malnutrition underlying noma. Provides nutrition support and feeding programs. Offers psychosocial support for disfigured children. Conducts noma prevention and awareness campaigns. Collaborates with WHO on noma elimination. Noma prevalent in poverty-stricken northern communities. 90% mortality without treatment, survivors face severe disfigurement and social stigma. Addresses challenges from poverty, malnutrition, limited awareness and late presentation. Critical for noma treatment, facial reconstruction and supporting affected children despite disease's neglected status and poverty context.

**Core Mandate:** To treat noma disease in children, provide facial reconstruction surgery, address malnutrition, prevent noma and support noma survivors' rehabilitation.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Treat noma (cancrum oris) disease in affected children
(b) Provide specialized care for neglected tropical disease
(c) Address severe malnutrition underlying noma
(d) Conduct plastic and reconstructive facial surgery
(e) Rebuild facial structures destroyed by noma
(f) Provide nutrition support and therapeutic feeding
(g) Offer psychosocial support for disfigured children
(h) Address stigma and social exclusion facing noma survivors
(i) Conduct noma prevention awareness campaigns
(j) Promote early detection and prompt treatment
(k) Collaborate with WHO on noma elimination programs
(l) Operate in noma-endemic Sokoto and northwestern Nigeria
(m) Address 90% mortality rate without treatment
(n) Provide long-term rehabilitation for survivors
(o) Support children's reintegration into communities
(p) Address poverty contributing to noma
(q) Combat malnutrition in vulnerable children
(r) Promote oral hygiene and disease prevention
(s) Address late presentation delaying treatment
(t) Advance noma treatment and prevention supporting malnourished children addressing neglected disease causing facial disfigurement in poverty contexts

## 595. NATIONAL BLOOD SERVICE COMMISSION (NBSC)

**MDA Code:** 521047001

National Blood Service Commission is federal agency managing blood transfusion services and ensuring safe blood supply. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to coordinate blood collection, testing, processing and distribution ensuring safe blood for medical procedures. Operates blood banks collecting blood from voluntary donors. Screens donated blood for HIV, Hepatitis B, Hepatitis C, syphilis and other infections. Processes blood into components (red cells, platelets, plasma). Distributes safe blood to hospitals nationwide. Promotes voluntary non-remunerated blood donation. Conducts blood donor recruitment and retention programs. Ensures blood safety through quality assurance. Operates National Blood Transfusion Service guidelines. Addresses blood shortages especially during emergencies. Collaborates with hospitals, blood banks and donor organizations. Addresses challenges from inadequate voluntary donation (many patients rely on family replacement donors), limited blood banks and cultural barriers. Safe blood transfusion critical for surgeries, maternal health, trauma care and treating blood disorders including sickle cell anemia prevalent in Nigeria. Aims to achieve 100% voluntary blood donation and eliminate blood-borne infection transmission.

**Core Mandate:** To ensure safe blood supply, coordinate blood transfusion services, promote voluntary blood donation, screen blood for infections and distribute quality blood products nationwide.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate national blood transfusion services and safe blood supply
(b) Operate blood banks collecting blood from donors
(c) Promote voluntary non-remunerated blood donation
(d) Conduct blood donor recruitment and retention campaigns
(e) Screen donated blood for HIV, Hepatitis B and C, and syphilis
(f) Test blood for malaria and other infectious diseases
(g) Process whole blood into components (red cells, platelets, plasma)
(h) Distribute safe blood and blood products to hospitals
(i) Ensure blood safety through quality assurance programs
(j) Operate National Blood Transfusion Service standards
(k) Address blood shortages during emergencies and disasters
(l) Maintain blood inventory and availability
(m) Promote blood donation awareness and education
(n) Address cultural barriers to blood donation
(o) Eliminate paid blood donation and family replacement
(p) Train blood bank personnel and transfusion specialists
(q) Monitor transfusion-transmitted infections
(r) Collaborate with hospitals on blood utilization
(s) Coordinate with WHO on blood safety standards
(t) Advance blood safety and availability ensuring adequate safe blood for surgeries, maternal care and treating blood disorders including sickle cell disease

## 658. NATIONAL OBSTETRIC FISTULA CENTRE, ABAKALIKI

**MDA Code:** 521048001

National Obstetric Fistula Centre Abakaliki is specialized federal hospital treating obstetric fistula in southeastern Nigeria. Located in Abakaliki, Ebonyi State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of national network of fistula treatment centers. Provides free surgical repair of obstetric fistula for southeastern women. Treats vesico-vaginal and recto-vaginal fistula from obstructed labor. Conducts complex fistula repair surgeries including revision cases. Offers holistic fistula care including medical, surgical and psychosocial support. Provides rehabilitation and continence training post-surgery. Addresses stigma and social isolation facing fistula patients. Supports survivors' social and economic reintegration. Trains healthcare providers on fistula prevention and management. Conducts outreach identifying fistula cases in rural communities. Promotes antenatal care and institutional delivery. Advocates for emergency obstetric care access preventing obstructed labor. Collaborates with UNFPA, international partners and NGOs. Southeastern Nigeria has lower fistula prevalence than north but cases still occur from rural deliveries, teenage pregnancy and poverty limiting healthcare access. Serves Ebonyi, Enugu, Abia, Imo and southeastern states. Critical for maternal health, preventing maternal morbidity and restoring affected women's dignity and social participation.

**Core Mandate:** To provide free obstetric fistula surgery, restore dignity, prevent fistula through healthcare access, and serve southeastern Nigeria women suffering from obstetric injuries.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide specialized obstetric fistula treatment for southeastern Nigeria
(b) Conduct free surgical repair of vesico-vaginal fistula
(c) Treat recto-vaginal fistula from obstructed labor complications
(d) Perform complex fistula repair including revision surgeries
(e) Offer holistic fistula care - medical, surgical and psychosocial
(f) Provide comprehensive pre-operative assessment
(g) Deliver post-operative rehabilitation and continence training
(h) Provide psychological support addressing emotional trauma
(i) Address stigma and social isolation facing patients
(j) Support survivors' social reintegration
(k) Promote economic empowerment for fistula survivors
(l) Train healthcare providers on fistula prevention and management
(m) Conduct community outreach identifying cases in rural areas
(n) Promote antenatal care and institutional delivery
(o) Advocate for emergency obstetric care access
(p) Address teenage pregnancy and early childbearing contributing to fistula
(q) Serve Ebonyi, Enugu, Abia, Imo and southeastern states
(r) Collaborate with UNFPA on fistula elimination
(s) Engage international fistula organizations and NGOs
(t) Advance maternal health preventing maternal morbidity restoring dignity for southeastern women through free surgery and comprehensive care addressing obstetric fistula from obstructed labor

## 656. NATIONAL OBSTETRIC FISTULA CENTRE, BAUCHI

**MDA Code:** 521048002

National Obstetric Fistula Centre Bauchi is specialized federal hospital treating obstetric fistula in northeastern Nigeria. Located in Bauchi, Bauchi State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of network of fistula centers addressing maternal health complications. Obstetric fistula results from prolonged obstructed labor causing devastating injury - hole between birth canal and bladder/rectum leading to constant urine/feces leakage. Provides free surgical repair of vesico-vaginal fistula (VVF) and recto-vaginal fistula (RVF). Conducts complex fistula surgeries including revision repairs. Offers comprehensive fistula care including pre-operative counseling and post-operative rehabilitation. Provides psychosocial support addressing trauma, stigma and social exclusion. Supports social reintegration for fistula survivors. Trains surgeons on fistula repair techniques. Conducts outreach programs identifying fistula cases in communities. Promotes safe childbirth and emergency obstetric care preventing fistula. Collaborates with UNFPA, Fistula Foundation and partners. Northeastern Nigeria has high fistula prevalence from early marriage, home deliveries and limited obstetric care access. Serves Bauchi, Gombe, Yobe and surrounding states. Critical for women's health, restoring dignity and preventing maternal morbidity.

**Core Mandate:** To provide free obstetric fistula repair, restore dignity to affected women, prevent fistula through safe childbirth, and serve northeastern Nigeria fistula patients.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide specialized treatment for obstetric fistula in northeastern Nigeria
(b) Conduct free surgical repair of vesico-vaginal fistula (VVF)
(c) Treat recto-vaginal fistula (RVF) from obstructed labor
(d) Perform complex fistula surgeries including revision cases
(e) Provide comprehensive pre-operative fistula assessment and counseling
(f) Deliver post-operative care and continence training
(g) Offer physical rehabilitation for fistula survivors
(h) Provide psychosocial support addressing trauma and stigma
(i) Support social reintegration reducing isolation
(j) Address social exclusion facing fistula patients
(k) Train surgeons on fistula repair surgical techniques
(l) Train nurses on fistula patient care and management
(m) Conduct community outreach identifying fistula cases
(n) Promote awareness on obstetric fistula prevention
(o) Advocate for skilled birth attendance and emergency obstetric care
(p) Address child marriage contributing to fistula
(q) Serve Bauchi, Gombe, Yobe and northeastern states
(r) Collaborate with UNFPA on fistula elimination programs
(s) Engage Fistula Foundation and international partners
(t) Advance women's health restoring dignity for northeastern Nigeria fistula patients through free surgery, rehabilitation and prevention addressing maternal morbidity from obstructed labor

## 657. NATIONAL OBSTETRIC FISTULA CENTRE, KATSINA

**MDA Code:** 521048003

National Obstetric Fistula Centre Katsina is specialized federal hospital treating obstetric fistula in northwestern Nigeria. Located in Katsina, Katsina State, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Part of national network addressing fistula crisis affecting thousands of women. Provides free surgical repair of vesico-vaginal fistula and recto-vaginal fistula for northwestern women. Conducts fistula surgeries restoring continence and dignity. Offers comprehensive fistula care from diagnosis through rehabilitation. Provides social and psychological support for traumatized patients. Addresses severe stigma facing fistula sufferers in conservative northern communities. Supports economic empowerment enabling survivors rebuild livelihoods. Northwestern Nigeria has particularly high fistula rates from early marriage (some girls married at 13-15), home deliveries and limited healthcare access. Serves Katsina, Kano, Jigawa, Zamfara and surrounding states. Critical for maternal health, dignity restoration and preventing preventable maternal morbidity.

## 631. NATIONAL HOSPITAL, ABUJA

**MDA Code:** 521049001

National Hospital Abuja is premier federal tertiary hospital and flagship healthcare institution. Located in Abuja, Federal Capital Territory, operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established as Nigeria's foremost tertiary referral hospital providing highest level specialist care. Provides comprehensive specialist services across all medical specialties. Operates as quaternary referral center for complex cases. Treats VIPs, government officials and general public. Provides cardiac surgery, neurosurgery, organ transplants and advanced procedures. Operates comprehensive cancer treatment center. Provides renal dialysis and kidney transplant services. Operates cardiac catheterization laboratory. Provides advanced diagnostic services including MRI, CT scan and nuclear medicine. Trains resident doctors across specialties. Collaborates with universities on medical education. Conducts clinical research on health challenges. Operates 600+ bed capacity. Faces challenges from overwhelming patient load, inadequate funding, equipment maintenance and balancing VIP care with public access. Serves as national medical reference facility. Presidential and top government officials receive care. Critical for specialized healthcare, medical training and serving as apex tertiary hospital for Nigeria.

**Core Mandate:** To provide tertiary and quaternary healthcare, deliver specialized medical services, train medical professionals, conduct research and serve as premier national referral hospital.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide tertiary and quaternary healthcare as national referral hospital
(b) Deliver comprehensive specialist medical services across all specialties
(c) Provide cardiac surgery and interventional cardiology
(d) Conduct neurosurgery for brain and spinal conditions
(e) Perform organ transplants including kidney transplants
(f) Provide comprehensive cancer treatment and oncology
(g) Operate renal dialysis center for kidney failure
(h) Deliver cardiac catheterization and interventional procedures
(9) Provide advanced surgical services across specialties
(j) Operate comprehensive diagnostic services (MRI, CT, nuclear medicine)
(k) Treat complex and rare medical conditions
(l) Provide VIP healthcare for President and government officials
(m) Deliver healthcare to general public alongside VIP services
(n) Train resident doctors through postgraduate medical programs
(o) Provide clinical training for medical students
(p) Train nurses, pharmacists and allied health professionals
(q) Conduct clinical research on health challenges
(r) Operate 600+ bed capacity serving Federal Capital
(s) Collaborate with universities and teaching hospitals
(t) Advance healthcare delivery and medical excellence providing Nigeria's highest-level tertiary care, specialized services and medical training as flagship national hospital

## 636. NATIONAL INSTITUTE FOR CANCER RESEARCH AND TREATMENT (NICRAT)

**MDA Code:** 521051001

National Institute for Cancer Research and Treatment is specialized federal cancer hospital and research institution. Operating under Federal Ministry of Health. Established to provide comprehensive cancer care, conduct cancer research and address Nigeria's growing cancer burden. Provides cancer diagnosis and staging services. Delivers chemotherapy for various cancers. Provides radiotherapy and radiation oncology. Conducts cancer surgeries including oncologic procedures. Offers palliative care for advanced cancer patients. Conducts cancer research on prevalent cancers in Nigeria. Researches cancer prevention and early detection. Provides cancer screening programs. Trains oncologists, cancer surgeons and radiation therapists. Promotes cancer awareness and education. Addresses common Nigerian cancers - breast cancer, cervical cancer, prostate cancer, liver cancer. Collaborates with international cancer centers and organizations. Faces challenges from limited radiotherapy machines (Nigeria has ~30 machines for 200M population vs. recommended 400+), expensive cancer drugs, late diagnosis and inadequate cancer infrastructure. Cancer incidence rising - estimated 100,000+ new cases annually. Critical for cancer control, specialized treatment and research addressing growing cancer crisis.

**Core Mandate:** To provide comprehensive cancer treatment, conduct cancer research, train oncology professionals, promote early detection and address Nigeria's cancer burden.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Provide comprehensive cancer diagnosis and treatment services
(b) Conduct cancer staging and diagnostic pathology
(c) Deliver chemotherapy for various cancer types
(d) Provide radiotherapy and radiation oncology services
(e) Conduct cancer surgeries and oncologic procedures
(f) Offer palliative care for advanced cancer patients
(g) Provide pain management and end-of-life care
(h) Conduct cancer research on prevalent Nigerian cancers
(i) Research breast cancer prevention and treatment
(j) Study cervical cancer and HPV vaccination
(k) Research prostate cancer affecting Nigerian men
(l) Study liver cancer linked to Hepatitis B
(m) Conduct cancer prevention and early detection research
(n) Provide cancer screening programs
(o) Train medical oncologists and clinical oncologists
(p) Train radiation therapists and cancer nurses
(q) Promote cancer awareness and education campaigns
(r) Address late diagnosis through community outreach
(s) Collaborate with international cancer organizations
(t) Advance cancer control and treatment addressing Nigeria's 100,000+ annual new cases despite limited radiotherapy infrastructure (30 vs. 400+ machines needed) and diagnostic challenges

## 670. NATIONAL PARKS HEADQUARTERS

**MDA Code:** 535002001

National Parks Headquarters is federal coordinating body overseeing Nigeria's national parks system. Operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Coordinates seven national parks protecting Nigeria's biodiversity and ecosystems. Oversees Kainji, Gashaka Gumti, Cross River, Chad Basin, Old Oyo, Yankari and Kamuku National Parks. Develops national park policies and management frameworks. Coordinates wildlife conservation across park system. Promotes eco-tourism and nature-based tourism. Manages park budgets and resources allocation. Coordinates anti-poaching enforcement in parks. Addresses human-wildlife conflicts around parks. Promotes environmental education and conservation awareness. Collaborates with international conservation organizations. Monitors endangered species in parks. Addresses park encroachment and illegal activities. Promotes community-based conservation around parks. Coordinates research on biodiversity and ecosystems. Faces challenges from inadequate funding, poaching, illegal logging, mining in parks and limited enforcement capacity. Nigeria's parks protect critical ecosystems, endangered elephants, chimpanzees and biodiversity. Critical for biodiversity conservation, ecosystem protection and coordinating national park system management.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate national parks system, oversee park management, promote wildlife conservation, develop eco-tourism, protect biodiversity and ensure effective parks administration.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate Nigeria's seven national parks system
(b) Oversee Kainji, Gashaka Gumti and Cross River National Parks
(c) Manage Chad Basin, Old Oyo, Yankari and Kamuku National Parks
(d) Develop national park policies and management frameworks
(e) Coordinate wildlife conservation across park system
(f) Protect endangered species including elephants and chimpanzees
(g) Promote eco-tourism and nature-based tourism development
(h) Manage park budgets and resource allocation
(i) Coordinate anti-poaching enforcement operations
(j) Address human-wildlife conflicts around park boundaries
(k) Promote environmental education and conservation awareness
(l) Collaborate with WWF, IUCN and international conservation bodies
(m) Monitor endangered species populations and ecosystems
(n) Address park encroachment, illegal logging and mining

## 793. OLD OYO NATIONAL PARK

**MDA Code:** 535004001

Old Oyo National Park is federal protected area conserving savanna ecosystems and historical sites. Operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Located in Oyo and Kwara States preserving savanna biodiversity and Old Oyo Empire ruins. Protects savanna woodland and grassland ecosystems. Conserves wildlife including buffalo, roan antelope and elephants. Preserves historical Old Oyo Empire archaeological sites. Promotes cultural and heritage tourism. Operates in north-central/southwestern borderlands. Addresses poaching and bushmeat hunting. Combats illegal grazing and cattle encroachment. Promotes ecotourism combining wildlife and history. Provides environmental education programs. Collaborates with local communities and traditional rulers. Addresses challenges from limited funding, poaching, farmer-herder conflicts and encroachment. Old Oyo Empire (16th-19th century) historical significance. Combines natural and cultural heritage. Critical for savanna conservation, wildlife protection and cultural heritage preservation despite funding and encroachment challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To protect Old Oyo savanna ecosystem and wildlife, preserve Old Oyo Empire historical sites, promote eco-cultural tourism and conserve natural and cultural heritage.

Okumu National Park is federal protected area conserving rainforest ecosystems in southern Nigeria. Operating under Federal Ministry of Environment as one of Nigeria's national parks. Operates in Edo State southern Nigeria. Promotes ecotourism and nature-based tourism. Provides canopy walkway tourist attraction. Protects forest biodiversity and endemic species. Addresses deforestation and illegal logging. Combats poaching and bushmeat hunting. Promotes environmental education and awareness. Collaborates with local communities on conservation. ## 660. NATIONAL OIL SPILL DETECTION AND RESPONSE AGENCY (NOSDRA)

**MDA Code:** 535015001

National Oil Spill Detection and Response Agency is federal regulatory agency preventing and responding to oil spills in Nigeria's oil-producing regions. Headquartered in Abuja with offices in Niger Delta, operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Established to regulate oil industry environmental compliance, detect oil spills and coordinate spill response. Detects and monitors oil spills in Niger Delta and offshore. Coordinates oil spill response and cleanup operations. Enforces oil industry environmental regulations (NESREA handles non-oil sectors). Ensures oil companies conduct Environmental Impact Assessments. Monitors pipeline integrity and leak detection systems. Imposes sanctions on companies causing oil spills. Coordinates oil spill compensation for affected communities. Promotes oil spill prevention and preparedness. Operates National Oil Spill Contingency Plan. Collaborates with oil companies on spill response. Addresses Niger Delta environmental degradation from decades of oil pollution - Ogoniland still severely contaminated despite cleanup promises. UNEP 2011 report documented extensive oil pollution requiring billions for remediation. Faces challenges from oil theft/bunkering causing spills, inadequate cleanup, limited enforcement capacity and industry non-compliance. Critical for environmental protection, Niger Delta cleanup and holding oil companies accountable for pollution.

**Core Mandate:** To detect and respond to oil spills, regulate oil industry environmental compliance, coordinate spill cleanup, protect Niger Delta environment and ensure oil pollution accountability.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Detect and monitor oil spills in Niger Delta and offshore waters
(b) Coordinate oil spill response and cleanup operations
(c) Regulate oil industry environmental compliance
(d) Enforce environmental regulations in oil and gas sector
(e) Ensure oil companies conduct Environmental Impact Assessments
(f) Monitor pipeline integrity and leak detection systems
(g) Inspect oil facilities for environmental compliance
(h) Impose sanctions and fines on companies causing oil spills
(i) Coordinate oil spill damage assessment
(j) Ensure oil spill compensation for affected communities
(k) Promote oil spill prevention and preparedness programs
(l) Operate National Oil Spill Contingency Plan
(m) Maintain oil spill response equipment and stockpiles
(n) Collaborate with oil companies on spill response protocols
(o) Address oil theft and illegal bunkering causing spills
(p) Monitor offshore oil production environmental impacts
(q) Coordinate Ogoniland cleanup following UNEP recommendations
(r) Address chronic oil pollution in Niger Delta
(s) Promote environmental restoration of polluted areas
(t) Advance Niger Delta environmental protection responding to oil spills, enforcing compliance and coordinating cleanup addressing decades of oil pollution requiring accountability from oil companies

## 623. NATIONAL ENVIRONMENTAL STANDARDS AND REGULATIONS ENFORCEMENT AGENCY (NESREA)

**MDA Code:** 535016001

National Environmental Standards and Regulations Enforcement Agency is federal regulatory agency enforcing environmental laws and regulations. Headquartered in Abuja with zonal offices nationwide, operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Established in 2007 to enforce environmental compliance excluding oil and gas sector (regulated by NOSDRA). Enforces environmental standards and pollution control. Conducts environmental inspections of industries and facilities. Monitors air quality and atmospheric pollution. Regulates water pollution and effluent discharge. Enforces waste management regulations for municipal and industrial waste. Controls noise pollution from industries and facilities. Regulates hazardous waste handling and disposal. Conducts environmental impact assessment compliance monitoring. Issues environmental sanctions and penalties for violations. Promotes environmental awareness and compliance. Addresses plastic pollution and non-biodegradable waste. Collaborates with state environmental agencies. Faces challenges from limited enforcement capacity, industrial non-compliance and inadequate waste management infrastructure. Environmental pollution severe in industrial cities. Critical for environmental protection, pollution control and ensuring industries comply with environmental standards protecting public health and ecosystems.

**Core Mandate:** To enforce environmental laws and regulations, control pollution, monitor compliance, sanction violators and protect Nigeria's environment and public health from pollution and environmental degradation.

(6) Control industrial waste disposal and hazardous waste

(h) Monitor plastic pollution and non-biodegradable waste

(n) Regulate e-waste and electronic equipment disposal
(o) Control ozone depleting substances

(q) Collaborate with state environmental protection agencies
(r) Address climate change and environmental degradation
(s) Regulate environmental quality in urban and industrial areas

## 582. NATIONAL AGENCY FOR GREAT GREEN WALL (NAGGW)

**MDA Code:** 535021001

National Agency for Great Green Wall is federal agency coordinating Nigeria's participation in Africa's Great Green Wall Initiative. Headquartered in Nigeria, operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Great Green Wall is pan-African initiative planting trees across Sahel from Senegal to Djibouti combating desertification. Nigeria participates covering 11 frontline states affected by desertification (Sokoto, Kebbi, Zamfara, Katsina, Kano, Jigawa, Yobe, Borno, Bauchi, Gombe, Adamawa). NAGGW coordinates tree planting, land restoration and sustainable land management. Combats desertification advancing southward from Sahara Desert. Restores degraded lands and vegetation cover. Promotes agroforestry and sustainable agriculture in Sahel zone. Engages communities in tree planting and conservation. Provides alternative livelihoods reducing pressure on forests. Monitors desertification and land degradation. Collaborates with Great Green Wall Africa initiative and Sahel countries. Addresses challenges from climate change, deforestation, overgrazing and inadequate funding. Critical for combating desertification threatening northern Nigeria agriculture and livelihoods.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate Great Green Wall Initiative in Nigeria, combat desertification, restore degraded lands, promote tree planting and support sustainable land management in Sahel zone.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate Nigeria's participation in Great Green Wall pan-African initiative
(b) Combat desertification in 11 northern frontline states
(c) Plant trees across Sahel zone creating green barrier
(d) Restore degraded lands and vegetation cover
(e) Promote agroforestry integrating trees with agriculture
(f) Engage communities in tree planting campaigns
(g) Provide alternative livelihoods reducing deforestation pressure
(h) Monitor desertification advancement from Sahara Desert
(i) Conduct land degradation assessments
(j) Promote sustainable land management practices
(k) Support sustainable agriculture in semi-arid regions
(l) Address overgrazing destroying vegetation
(m) Promote soil conservation and erosion control
(n) Develop community woodlots and forest reserves
(o) Conduct environmental education on desertification
(p) Collaborate with Great Green Wall Africa Secretariat
(q) Coordinate with Sahel countries on cross-border initiatives
(r) Access climate finance for land restoration
(s) Monitor climate change impacts on northern ecosystems
(t) Advance environmental sustainability and climate resilience combating desertification threatening agriculture, water resources and livelihoods in northern Nigeria's Sahel zone

## 593. NATIONAL BIOSAFETY MANAGEMENT AGENCY (NBMA)

**MDA Code:** 535022001

National Biosafety Management Agency is federal regulatory agency overseeing biotechnology, genetically modified organisms (GMOs) and biosafety. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Environment. Established by National Biosafety Management Agency Act 2015 to regulate modern biotechnology applications ensuring safety. Regulates GMO research, field trials, importation and commercial release. Conducts biosafety risk assessments for GMO applications. Issues permits for confined field trials of GMO crops. Approves commercial release of GMO crops meeting safety standards. Regulates GMO imports including food aid and agricultural commodities. Enforces biosafety protocols and containment measures. Monitors environmental and health impacts of GMOs. Promotes public awareness on biotechnology and GMOs. Collaborates with research institutes conducting GMO research. Implements Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety. Addresses challenges from public skepticism about GMOs, limited capacity and conflicting interests between innovation and precaution. Nigeria has approved GMO cowpea (beans) and cotton for cultivation. Critical for regulating biotechnology development balancing innovation with safety and environmental protection.

**Core Mandate:** To regulate biotechnology and GMOs, ensure biosafety compliance, conduct risk assessments, approve GMO releases and protect human health and environment from biotechnology risks.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Regulate modern biotechnology and genetically modified organisms (GMOs)
(b) Conduct biosafety risk assessments for GMO applications
(c) Issue permits for GMO research and confined field trials
(d) Approve commercial release of GMO crops meeting safety standards
(e) Regulate importation of GMO products including food and feed
(f) Enforce biosafety protocols and containment measures
(g) Monitor environmental impacts of GMO releases
(h) Assess human health risks from GMO crops and products
(i) Regulate GMO handling, transport and storage
(j) Conduct post-release monitoring of commercial GMOs
(k) Promote public awareness on biotechnology and biosafety
(l) Collaborate with research institutes on GMO development
(m) Implement Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety obligations
(n) Maintain National Biosafety Database on GMO applications
(o) Enforce labeling requirements for GMO products
(p) Address illegal GMO releases and violations
(q) Provide technical guidance on biosafety to stakeholders
(r) Coordinate with NAFDAC on GMO food safety
(s) Engage international biosafety networks and agencies
(t) Advance safe biotechnology development balancing innovation with precaution ensuring GMO applications protect human health and environment while supporting agricultural productivity

## 622. NATIONAL EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT AGENCY (NEMA)

**MDA Code:** 554002001

National Emergency Management Agency is federal agency coordinating disaster management, emergency response and humanitarian assistance. Headquartered in Abuja with zonal and operational offices nationwide, operating under Office of President (previously under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs). Established to coordinate disaster response, prevention and mitigation. Responds to natural disasters including floods, erosion and droughts. Manages response to humanitarian emergencies from conflicts and displacement. Coordinates relief distribution to disaster-affected populations. Operates IDP camps and emergency shelters. Provides search and rescue services during disasters. Conducts disaster risk reduction and preparedness programs. Collaborates with state emergency management agencies (SEMAs). Coordinates international humanitarian assistance and donor support. Responds to annual flooding affecting millions (2012 floods displaced 2+ million, 2022 floods affected 4+ million across 34 states). Addresses fire disasters, building collapses and transportation accidents. Faces challenges from climate change increasing disaster frequency, inadequate early warning systems and limited resources. Critical for disaster response, humanitarian assistance and protecting Nigerians from recurrent emergencies especially devastating annual floods.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate disaster management and emergency response, provide humanitarian assistance, conduct disaster preparedness, mitigate disasters and protect Nigerians from emergencies and natural disasters.

**Statutory Functions:**

(b) Respond to natural disasters including floods and erosion
(c) Manage humanitarian emergencies from conflicts and insurgency
(d) Coordinate relief distribution to disaster-affected populations
(e) Provide food, shelter and non-food items to displaced persons
(f) Operate IDP camps and emergency shelters
(g) Provide search and rescue services during disasters
(h) Respond to annual flooding affecting millions of Nigerians
(i) Address fire disasters and building collapses
(j) Respond to transportation accidents and mass casualty incidents
(k) Conduct disaster risk reduction programs
(l) Promote disaster preparedness and early warning systems

(n) Coordinate Local Emergency Management Agencies (LEMAs)
(o) Coordinate international humanitarian assistance
(p) Manage donor support and relief supplies
(q) Conduct damage and needs assessments after disasters
(r) Promote climate change adaptation reducing disaster vulnerability
(s) Conduct public education on disaster preparedness
(t) Advance disaster resilience and emergency response managing recurrent flooding (2012: 2M displaced, 2022: 4M+ affected) and humanitarian crises protecting Nigerians from disasters

## 685. NATIONAL SOCIAL INVESTMENT OFFICE (NSIO)

**MDA Code:** 554003001

National Social Investment Office manages federal social safety net programs for poor and vulnerable Nigerians. Operating under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs. Established to coordinate National Social Investment Programme (NSIP) - Africa's largest social protection program. Operates N-Power program employing 500,000+ graduates as teachers, health workers and agriculture extension agents. Manages Conditional Cash Transfer (CCT) providing monthly stipends to poorest households. Operates Government Enterprise and Empowerment Programme (GEEP) providing micro-loans to traders and artisans. Runs Home Grown School Feeding Programme feeding 9+ million primary school children daily. Promotes financial inclusion for vulnerable populations. Addresses poverty and unemployment especially among youth. Provides social protection during economic hardships. Collaborates with states on program implementation. Uses technology for beneficiary identification and payment. Addresses challenges from payment delays, ghost beneficiaries, limited coverage (millions still in poverty), sustainability concerns and implementation gaps. NSIP launched 2016 as poverty alleviation and social protection intervention. Critical for poverty reduction, social protection and supporting vulnerable Nigerians during economic difficulties.

**Core Mandate:** To manage social investment programs, operate N-Power employment, deliver cash transfers, provide school feeding and support poverty reduction and social protection.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Manage National Social Investment Programme (NSIP) - Africa's largest
(b) Operate N-Power program employing 500,000+ graduates
(c) Deploy N-Power volunteers as teachers in schools
(d) Place N-Power beneficiaries as health workers in PHCs
(e) Deploy agricultural extension agents through N-Power Agro
(f) Manage Conditional Cash Transfer (CCT) for poorest households
(g) Provide monthly stipends (N5,000-N10,000) to vulnerable families
(h) Operate Government Enterprise and Empowerment Programme (GEEP)
(i) Provide micro-loans to traders, artisans and market women
(j) Run Home Grown School Feeding Programme
(k) Feed 9+ million primary school pupils daily
(l) Promote financial inclusion for vulnerable populations
(m) Address poverty affecting millions of Nigerians
(n) Tackle youth unemployment through job creation
(o) Provide social protection during economic hardships
(p) Collaborate with states on program implementation
(q) Use technology for beneficiary identification
(r) Address payment delays and ghost beneficiary fraud
(s) Monitor program impact on poverty reduction

## 773. NORTH EAST DEVELOPMENT COMMISSION (NEDC)

**MDA Code:** 554004001

North East Development Commission is federal intervention agency rebuilding Boko Haram-devastated northeastern region. Operating under Federal Ministry covering six states: Adamawa, Bauchi, Borno, Gombe, Taraba, Yobe. Established 2017 to address Boko Haram insurgency devastation. Rebuilds infrastructure destroyed by insurgency - schools, hospitals, roads. Provides humanitarian assistance to IDPs and affected communities. Resettles displaced persons and returnees. Reconstructs northeastern economy post-conflict. Supports livelihoods and economic recovery. Provides education for out-of-school children. Builds healthcare facilities replacing destroyed hospitals. Addresses trauma and psychosocial support. Promotes peacebuilding and reconciliation. Collaborates with military on stabilization. Addresses ongoing insecurity challenges - insurgency continues despite military gains. Over 35,000 deaths, millions displaced, infrastructure destroyed. Faces challenges from active insurgency, limited access to liberated areas, enormous reconstruction needs and coordination gaps. NEDC represents hope for northeastern recovery. Critical for post-conflict reconstruction, humanitarian response and rebuilding devastated region despite ongoing security challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To rebuild Boko Haram-devastated northeast, provide humanitarian assistance, reconstruct infrastructure, support IDPs, promote recovery and address insurgency impacts.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Rebuild northeastern Nigeria devastated by Boko Haram insurgency
(b) Develop six northeastern states (Adamawa, Bauchi, Borno, Gombe, Taraba, Yobe)
(c) Reconstruct infrastructure destroyed by insurgency
(d) Rebuild schools destroyed or damaged by terrorists
(e) Reconstruct hospitals and healthcare facilities
(f) Repair roads and bridges damaged in conflict
(g) Provide humanitarian assistance to IDPs and affected communities
(h) Support resettlement of displaced persons and returnees
(i) Provide livelihood support and economic recovery programs
(j) Support agriculture revival in post-conflict areas
(k) Provide education for millions of out-of-school children
(l) Address trauma and provide psychosocial support
(m) Promote peacebuilding and community reconciliation
(n) Collaborate with military on stabilization efforts
(o) Address ongoing insecurity in liberated areas
(p) Respond to 35,000+ deaths and millions displaced
(q) Navigate active insurgency limiting access
(r) Coordinate reconstruction with enormous needs
(s) Address limited funding relative to devastation scale
(t) Advance northeastern recovery and reconstruction despite ongoing Boko Haram threats rebuilding after 35,000+ deaths and massive displacement

## 612. NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR REFUGEES, MIGRANTS AND INTERNALLY DISPLACED PERSONS (NCFRMI)

**MDA Code:** 554005001

National Commission for Refugees, Migrants and Internally Displaced Persons is federal agency managing refugee affairs, migration and internal displacement. Headquartered in Abuja, operating under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs. Established to coordinate protection and assistance for refugees, asylum seekers and internally displaced persons (IDPs). Manages refugee camps and settlements hosting refugees from neighboring countries. Provides protection and assistance to Cameroonian refugees fleeing Anglophone crisis. Coordinates assistance for internally displaced persons from Boko Haram insurgency (estimated 2+ million IDPs in northeastern Nigeria). Manages IDP camps in Borno, Adamawa and Yobe. Processes asylum applications and refugee status determination. Collaborates with UNHCR on refugee protection. Provides humanitarian assistance including shelter, food and healthcare. Promotes durable solutions including voluntary repatriation, local integration and resettlement. Addresses challenges from protracted displacement, inadequate funding and camp conditions. Boko Haram insurgency created massive internal displacement since 2009. Northwestern banditry displacing additional populations. Critical for humanitarian response, displacement crisis management and protecting displaced populations.

**Core Mandate:** To protect and assist refugees, asylum seekers and IDPs; manage displacement response; coordinate humanitarian assistance; and promote durable solutions for displaced populations.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Coordinate protection and assistance for refugees and asylum seekers
(b) Manage refugee camps and settlements in Nigeria
(c) Process asylum applications and refugee status determination
(d) Protect Cameroonian refugees fleeing Anglophone crisis
(e) Coordinate assistance for 2+ million internally displaced persons
(f) Manage IDP camps in Borno, Adamawa and Yobe States
(g) Provide humanitarian assistance including shelter and food
(h) Deliver healthcare and psychosocial support to displaced persons
(i) Promote education for refugee and IDP children
(j) Support livelihoods and economic empowerment for displaced
(k) Collaborate with UNHCR on refugee protection programs
(l) Coordinate with humanitarian organizations (ICRC, NGOs)
(m) Promote voluntary repatriation of refugees
(n) Support local integration of refugees in host communities
(o) Facilitate resettlement to third countries
(p) Address protracted displacement from Boko Haram
(q) Respond to new displacement from northwestern banditry
(r) Monitor and improve IDP camp conditions
(s) Prevent gender-based violence in displacement settings
(t) Advance humanitarian protection for displaced populations managing Boko Haram displacement crisis and refugee influx ensuring assistance and durable solutions
