## 700. NATIONAL AGENCY FOR THE PROHIBITION OF TRAFFICKING IN PERSONS (NAPTIP)

**MDA Code:** 554006001

National Agency for the Prohibition of Trafficking in Persons is federal law enforcement agency combating human trafficking and modern slavery. Headquartered in Abuja with zonal offices, operating under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs. Established to prevent trafficking, prosecute traffickers and protect victims. Investigates human trafficking cases and trafficking networks. Arrests and prosecutes human traffickers and smugglers. Rescues trafficking victims from exploitation. Provides shelter and rehabilitation for rescued victims. Addresses sex trafficking and forced prostitution. Combats child trafficking and child labor exploitation. Addresses forced labor and domestic servitude. Prevents trafficking to Europe (Libya route), Gulf States and within Nigeria. Conducts public awareness on trafficking dangers. Collaborates with immigration on border trafficking prevention. Works with IOM and international anti-trafficking organizations. Prosecutes trafficking cases in courts. Provides victim counseling and reintegration support. Addresses root causes - poverty, unemployment, irregular migration aspirations. Nigeria source, transit and destination for trafficking. Thousands trafficked annually, many perish in Sahara/Mediterranean attempting to reach Europe. Critical for victim protection, trafficker prosecution and preventing human trafficking destroying lives of vulnerable Nigerians especially women and children.

**Core Mandate:** To combat human trafficking, prosecute traffickers, rescue and rehabilitate victims, prevent trafficking and protect vulnerable Nigerians from modern slavery and exploitation.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Combat human trafficking and modern slavery in Nigeria
(b) Investigate human trafficking cases and criminal networks
(c) Arrest and prosecute human traffickers and smugglers
(d) Rescue trafficking victims from exploitation
(e) Provide shelter and safe houses for rescued victims
(f) Address sex trafficking and forced prostitution
(g) Combat child trafficking and child labor exploitation
(h) Address forced labor and domestic servitude
(i) Prevent trafficking to Europe via Libya and Mediterranean routes
(j) Combat trafficking to Gulf States and Middle East
(k) Address internal trafficking within Nigeria
(l) Conduct public awareness campaigns on trafficking dangers
(m) Collaborate with Immigration Service on border prevention
(n) Work with IOM on victim repatriation and assistance
(o) Prosecute trafficking cases in federal courts
(p) Provide victim counseling and psychosocial support
(q) Support victim reintegration and livelihood programs
(r) Address root causes including poverty and unemployment
(s) Prevent irregular migration fueling trafficking
(t) Advance victim protection prosecuting traffickers preventing human trafficking protecting vulnerable Nigerians especially women and children from exploitation and modern slavery

## 607. NATIONAL COMMISSION FOR PERSONS WITH DISABILITIES (NCPWD)

**MDA Code:** 554007001

National Commission for Persons with Disabilities is federal agency protecting rights and promoting welfare of persons with disabilities. Established by Discrimination Against Persons with Disabilities (Prohibition) Act 2018, operating under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs. Created to enforce disability rights, prevent discrimination and ensure inclusion. Enforces Discrimination Against Persons with Disabilities Act prohibiting discrimination. Promotes accessibility in public buildings, transportation and facilities. Advocates for inclusive education for children with disabilities. Supports employment and economic empowerment of persons with disabilities. Addresses stigma and discrimination against disabled persons. Promotes disability awareness and rights education. Monitors compliance with disability laws and regulations. Investigates disability discrimination complaints. Collaborates with disability organizations and advocacy groups. Promotes assistive devices and rehabilitation services. Addresses challenges from limited enforcement, inadequate accessibility infrastructure and persistent discrimination. Nigeria has estimated 25+ million persons with disabilities facing barriers to education, employment and social participation. Critical for disability rights, inclusion and ensuring persons with disabilities enjoy equal rights and opportunities.

**Core Mandate:** To protect rights of persons with disabilities, enforce anti-discrimination laws, promote accessibility and inclusion, and ensure equal opportunities for disabled Nigerians.

## 787. OFFICE OF THE SENIOR SPECIAL ASSISTANT TO THE PRESIDENT ON MDGs (OSSAP-MDGs)

**MDA Code:** 554008001

Office of the Senior Special Assistant on MDGs coordinated Nigeria's Millennium Development Goals implementation. Operating under Office of President. Established to coordinate MDG achievement, manage MDG-related programs and report on progress. MDGs (2000-2015) replaced by Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) 2015. Office likely transitioned to SDGs coordination. Coordinated Nigeria's MDG implementation across sectors. Managed MDG-related development programs and projects. Reported on Nigeria's progress toward eight MDGs (poverty, education, health, gender, child mortality, maternal health, diseases, environment). Coordinated with MDAs on MDG targets. Accessed international MDG funding and support. Promoted poverty reduction and social development. Coordinated health sector MDG programs. Supported education access and gender equality initiatives. Addressed challenges from limited MDG achievement - Nigeria missed most MDG targets by 2015. Poverty remained high, maternal/child mortality excessive, education gaps persisted. Office represents development coordination efforts. Now likely focused on SDGs implementation post-2015.

**Core Mandate:** To coordinate MDG/SDG implementation, manage development programs, report progress on goals and support poverty reduction and social development targets.

(a) Coordinate Nigeria's Millennium Development Goals implementation
(b) Transition to Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) coordination post-2015

(f) Access international development funding and support
(g) Promote poverty reduction and social development

(p) Tackle persistent poverty rates
(q) Address excessive maternal and child mortality
(r) Close education enrollment and quality gaps
(s) Coordinate development partners and donors
(t) Advance development goals coordination despite limited MDG achievement transitioning to SDGs implementation

## 683. NATIONAL SENIOR CITIZENS CENTRE

**MDA Code:** 554009001

National Senior Citizens Centre is federal agency promoting welfare, rights and development of elderly Nigerians. Operating under Federal Ministry of Humanitarian Affairs. Established to address aging population needs, protect elderly rights and promote active aging. Promotes elderly welfare and social protection. Advocates for senior citizens' rights and dignity. Addresses elder abuse, neglect and abandonment. Promotes pension access and retirement income security. Supports elderly healthcare and geriatric services. Operates senior citizens homes and care facilities. Promotes intergenerational solidarity and respect for elders. Conducts research on aging and elderly issues. Promotes productive aging and elderly employment. Addresses isolation and loneliness among elderly. Provides social activities and elderly clubs. Collaborates with pension agencies on retirees' welfare. Addresses challenges from limited elderly care infrastructure, pension arrears affecting retirees, inadequate geriatric healthcare and cultural shifts weakening traditional family care. Nigeria's aging population growing - life expectancy increasing, elderly population estimated 5-7 million. Traditional family care systems weakening with urbanization. Critical for elderly welfare, dignified aging and ensuring seniors' rights and wellbeing.

**Core Mandate:** To promote senior citizens' welfare, protect elderly rights, address elder abuse, support active aging and ensure dignified life for elderly Nigerians.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Promote welfare and social protection for senior citizens
(b) Advocate for elderly rights and dignity
(c) Address elder abuse, neglect and abandonment
(d) Protect elderly from financial exploitation
(e) Promote pension access and retirement income security
(f) Support elderly healthcare and geriatric services
(g) Operate senior citizens homes and residential care facilities
(h) Provide day care centers for elderly
(i) Promote intergenerational solidarity and respect for elders
(j) Conduct research on aging population and elderly needs
(k) Promote productive aging and elderly employment opportunities
(l) Address social isolation and loneliness among elderly
(m) Provide social activities, clubs and recreational programs
(n) Promote age-friendly communities and infrastructure
(o) Collaborate with pension agencies on retirees' welfare
(p) Address pension arrears affecting elderly retirees
(q) Promote elderly participation in community development
(r) Support traditional family care for elderly
(s) Address urbanization impact on elderly care systems
(t) Advance elderly welfare ensuring dignified aging for 5-7M elderly Nigerians addressing abuse, healthcare needs and pension access despite infrastructure gaps

---

## 699. NATIONAL YOUTH SERVICE CORPS (NYSC)

**MDA Code:** 555002001

National Youth Service Corps is federal scheme deploying university graduates for one-year national service. Operating under Federal Ministry of Youth and Sports Development. Established 1973 post-civil war to promote national unity, integration and development. Mobilizes Nigerian graduates for mandatory one-year service. Deploys corps members (corpers) to states different from their origin promoting national integration. Operates orientation camps providing paramilitary training. Posts graduates to schools as teachers, hospitals as health workers, communities as development agents. Promotes national unity and ethnic integration post-civil war. Provides N33,000 monthly allowance to corps members. Issues Certificate of National Service required for federal employment. Operates Skills Acquisition and Entrepreneurship Development (SAED). Promotes youth empowerment and self-employment. Coordinates Community Development Service (CDS). Addresses insecurity affecting corps members - some states dangerous for deployment, kidnapping risks, Boko Haram-affected areas. Relocation requests increase due to insecurity. NYSC once unifying institution now challenged by security concerns. Over 3 million Nigerians have served since 1973. Critical for national integration, youth development and post-graduation transition despite growing security challenges.

**Core Mandate:** To mobilize graduates for national service, promote national unity and integration, deploy corps members for development, empower youth and build national cohesion.

**Statutory Functions:**
(a) Mobilize Nigerian university graduates for mandatory one-year service
(b) Deploy corps members to states different from origin
(c) Promote national unity and ethnic integration post-civil war
(d) Operate orientation camps providing paramilitary training
(e) Post graduates to schools as teachers nationwide
(f) Deploy corps members to hospitals and health facilities
(g) Assign corpers to communities as development agents
(h) Provide N33,000 monthly allowance to corps members
(i) Issue Certificate of National Service for federal employment
(j) Operate Skills Acquisition and Entrepreneurship Development (SAED)
(k) Promote youth empowerment and self-employment skills
(l) Coordinate Community Development Service (CDS) projects
(m) Organize corps members for national development activities
(n) Address insecurity affecting deployment safety
(o) Manage relocation requests from dangerous states
(p) Exclude Boko Haram-affected areas from deployment
(q) Promote corps members' welfare and security
(r) Coordinate with host communities and employers
(s) Build national cohesion across ethnic and religious lines
(t) Advance national integration through youth service program deploying 3M+ graduates since 1973 promoting unity despite growing security challenges affecting deployment
